MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2021-01-11 | CLIP_ID: 783 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 783

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 11, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:0 0 p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center and via electronic conference meeting .  Attending 
electronic conference meeting  were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. 
Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, 
Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon request by  Mr. Cook , and pursuant to support 
received by Mr . Johnson and Mr. Roybal, agenda item # 23(a) was removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 28, 2020.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from the Sine Die Session  of the Governing Body on January 4, 2021.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 4, 2021.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located southeast of the 
intersection of High Plains Road and South Greeley Highway.  Charles Bloom, Director of 
Planning and Development, explained t hey had received a petition to annex the land , provided 
information on the property  and reviewed requirements of and compliance with annexation 
conditions contained in state statutes.  Comment was made related to taking precautions to avoid 
any potential for future flooding in the area of land to be annexed.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom 
provided information on public safety services that would be utilized for the annexation area. There 
being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins 
closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southeast of the intersection of Powderhouse Road and East Carlson Street.  Reporting for 
Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
   
Mr. White. Concern was expressed related to traffic in the area of Carlson and Powderhouse and 
along the east side access to Converse Ave.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4337) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential 
to P Public District for a tract of land generally located southeast of the intersection of 
Powderhouse Road and East Carlson Street .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. (#4338) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
define, establish permissibility, and create specific use standards for the use ‘Mini Storage’ .  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4339) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.04.020, Mayor -Election-Term of 
Office, of Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the 
Municipal Code of the city of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4340) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of and adjacent to South Greeley Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch 
Road – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential 
to AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial for land located east of and adjacent to South Greeley 
Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch Road – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI (Light Industrial) to MR (Medium -Density 
Residential) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Pebrican Ave nue and 
East 2nd Street – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne, changing the zoning classification from MR (Medium -Density Residential) to CB 
(Community Business) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Carbon 
Avenue and East 13th Street – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne)   
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Coyote 
Ridge, a plat of a portion of the northwest quarter of Section 20, Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
   
Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of Powderhouse Road and East Carlson Street).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6117) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Bison 
Business Park, a plat of portions of  Sections 28, 29, and 32, Township 13 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6 th P.M., City of Cheyenne,  Laramie County, Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to 
South Greeley Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch Road) – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sig n a final plat for 
Sweetgrass, 5th Filing, a subdivision of a portion of the N½ of Section 16, Township 13 North, 
Range 66 West of the 6 th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of 
East College Drive and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive  -- referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting an amendment to the Future Land Use Map in PlanCheyenne, 
amending the land use category for property generally located east of South Greeley Highway at 
Terry Ranch Road from Agricultural and Mixed-Use Employment to Industrial and amending the 
Urban Service Boundary and Natural and Cultural Resources Area -- referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA]  Preliminary Plat for Campstool Addition, 5 th Filing, generally located north of Venture 
Drive/Interstate 80 Service Road east of Industry Drive – referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
[CA] Letter of Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and the United 
States Department of Justice, Drug Enforcement Administration, for locating and eradicating illicit 
cannabis, and investigating and prosecuting those cases – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, C heyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization, and West Edge Collective LLC, for FY 2021 MPO Public Engagement 
and Web Support Services (MPO Federal funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and  GLM Design Group 
for the submission of two Building Resilient Infrastructure and Communities (BRIC) Capability - 
and Capacity- Building Funding grant applications to FEMA for the City of Cheyenne (2019-2022 
1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Contract Modification #2 to Contract # 6927 between Wyoming Homeland Security and 
the City of Cheyenne extending the term of the grant through August 31, 2021 (DHS Grand Code:  
EMW-2018-SS-00065).  Byron Mathews, Cheyenne Fire  and Rescue Division Chief, provided 
information on the modification stating it can’t be submitted due to the original grant application 
not being filed timely .  Mr. Mathews advised staff requests denial of the proposed Contract 
Modification and that staff will resubmit to meet grant funding compliance requirements.  Upon 
inquiry, Mr. Mathews advised the remaining grant fund balance is $5,000.00.  Upon inquiry, City 
Clerk Kris Jones, explained that as the agenda item received a recommendation for approval from 
   
the Finance Committee, the Chair should advise the Governing Body of that motion.  Ms. Jones 
further advised that the motion to approve could either die by lack of a second or be voted down 
as recommended by staff.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion failed 
with all members of the governing body voting “no” with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting 
“yes”. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract # 7206 between the City of Cheyenne and Inman 
Roofing, Inc., to replace wood nailer on  Cheyenne Aquatic Center roof (General Fund -CIP 
Account) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-9-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and 4 Quarters 
Excavation & Development for the East Cheyenne Community Park -Parking Lot and Trailhead 
Construction Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King Jr. Day Holiday on Monday, 
January 18, 2021, Finance Committee will meet at Noon on  Tuesday, January 19, 2021 via 
electronic conference meeting and Public Services Committee will meet at Noon  on Wednesday, 
January 20, 2021 via electronic conference meeting.   
 
 Under other business:  
  
 Mr. Johnson requested information related to the abandoned vehicle program in terms of 
which officer he should work with and obtaining the vehicle auction schedule. He also advised he 
had received complaints related to traffic enforcement in the downtown area, specifically CSO 
vehicles backing up traffic when tending to parking violations. Acting Police Chief Nathan Buseck 
provided information on the county run tow lot noting the number of vehicles can be fluid as the 
county also utilizes the lot and advised the auction schedule will be provided.  He explained there 
is a group email list for CSO’s that should be utilized.  Acting Chief Buseck advised he will meet 
with the Parking Manager about the downtown traffic enforcement concerns.    
 
 Mr. Johnson commented on a concern he had received from a constituent related to trash 
removal and street sweeping services occurring on the same day and requested an updat e on the 
Taft & Continental school zone traffic study that was done a few years ago.  Vicki Nemecek, 
Director of Public Works, explained street sweeping doesn’t typically occur on trash days, but if a 
request or work order is received, both services may occur. Ms. Nemecek advised she would 
discuss the school zone traffic study with the Engineering Department. 
 
 Mr. Johnson  commented on city staff procedures related to C OVID-19 cases.  Mayor 
Collins advised he has been in contact with the Laramie County Environmental Health Department 
and department directors and information will be forthcoming. 
 
 
   
 Dr. Aldrich advised Ward 3 council members are hosting a “Coffee with Council” event at 
Dazbog Coffee on Pershing on January 16, 2021. 
 
 Mr. Laybour n complemented everyon e who has been involved with m oving council 
meetings to the Civic Center .  He further expressed appreciation to  former Police Chief  Brian 
Kozak and former Fire Chief Greg Hoggatt, recognizing them for their outstanding service to the 
city. 
 
  There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:33 p.m.  
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
  
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  January 16, 2021

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2021-01-25 | CLIP_ID: 784 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 784

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 25, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: 
MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich , Bryan Cook, Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited.  [Clerk’s note:  
Mr. Esquibel attended the meeting via electronically] 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mayor Collins noted agenda item #17(a) was inadvertently 
placed on the consent agenda. Upon request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support received by 
Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Cook, agenda item # 17(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Segrave moved to approve  remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Votin g 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 11, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of and adjacent to South Greeley Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch 
Road.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential 
to AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial for land located east of and adjacent to South Greeley 
Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch Road.  Eric Crosby inquired on the industrial 
use planned for the land.  Brad Emmons , AVI PC, agent f or the applicant, advised the proposed 
zone will allow the land to be used for a data center project, noting it is not overall industrial in 
nature.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI (Light Industrial) to MR (Medium -Density 
Residential) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Pebrican Avenue and 
East 2 nd Street.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. 
Segrave declared a conflict of interest due to a business relationship  and did not participate in  
   
discussion or voting.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Segrave who didn’t vote.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne, changing the zoning classification from MR (Medium -Density Residential) to CB 
(Community Business) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Carbon 
Avenue and East 13 th Street.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  
Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Bison 
Business Park, a plat of portions of Sections 28, 29, and 32, Township 13 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6 th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to 
South Greeley Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch Road).  Dr. Rinne moved to 
adopt, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to February 8, 2021, seconded by 
Mr. Cook.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a final plat for 
Sweetgrass, 5th Filing, a subdivision of a portion of the N½ of Section 16, Township 13 North, 
Range 66 West of the 6 th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of 
East College Drive and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt with revised final 
plat map dated January 19, 2021, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6118) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting an amendment to the Future Land Use Map in PlanCheyenne, 
amending the land use category for property generally located east of South Greeley Highway at 
Terry Ranch Road from Agricultural and Mixed-Use Employment to Industrial and amending the 
Urban Service Boundary and Natural and Cultural Resources Area.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6119) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant Application under the Assistance to Firefighters Grant Program, on behalf of 
the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of purchasing a fire engine – referred 
to Finance Committee.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal advised the resolution had 
been reviewed under other items for the agenda at the January 19, 202 1 Finance Committee 
meeting, therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Cook .  Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Interim  Chief John Kopper  provided 
information on the resolution and explained they are requesting a  grant to replace  the current 
Engine #1 which is in dire need of replacement and noted the grant requires a 10% match.  Upon 
inquiry, Interim Chief Kopper explaine d it is recommended that front line engines should be no 
older than 10 years  old.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#6120) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant Application under the Assistance to Firefighters Grant Program, on behalf of 
the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of purchasing new automotive 
   
extrication equipment for all front line apparatus for Cheyenne Fire Rescue  – referred to Finance 
Committee.  Mr. Roybal advised the resolution had been reviewed under other items for the agenda 
at the January 19, 2021 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City Code 
have been met.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Cheyenne Fire and Rescue 
Interim Chief John Kopper explained the grant application is a joint regional application and is for 
extraction equipment.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6121) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Office on 
Violence Against Women OVW-2021-37003 Improving Criminal Justice Responses to Domestic 
Violence, Dating Violence, Sexual Assault and Stalking Grant Program CFDA Number 16.590 -
Grants to Encourage Arrests Policies and Enforcement of Protection Orders Program – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 Preliminary Plat for Campstool Addition, 5 th Filing, generally located north of Venture 
Drive/Interstate 80 Service Road east of In dustry Drive.  Upon inquiry, Kris Jones , City Clerk, 
explained item #15 is on the consent agenda for referral to the Finance Committee.  Dr. Rinne 
moved to acknowledge Planning Commission’s recommendation to approve with Planning 
Commission recommendations #1 – 3, seconded by Mr. Segrave.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. 
 
 Letter of Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and the United 
States Department of Justice, Drug Enforcement Administration, for locating and eradicating illicit 
cannabis, and investigating and prosecuting those cases.  Sergeant Tim Meyrick, Cheyenne Police 
Department, advised the Police Department is in support of the agreement and explained the funds 
can be used for  overtime, training and approved equipment purchases.   Upon inquiry, Sergeant 
Meyrick explained the annual dollar amount for cannabis enforcement doesn’t usually exceed the 
agreement amount.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in the amount of $65,000.00, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Upon further inquiry, Sergeant Meyrick explained the primary purpose of the agreement 
is for cannabis eradication, but it can apply to other types of illegal substances that may be 
encountered during cannabis eradication .  Comment was made noting cannabis is legal in some 
form in 39 states.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#7274) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenn e, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization, and West Edge Collective LLC, for FY 2021 MPO Public Engagement 
and Web Support Services (MPO Federal funds) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an 
amount not to exceed $31,200.00). (#7275) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and GLM Design Group 
for the submission of two Building Resilient Infrastructure and Communities (BRIC) Capability - 
and Capacity- Building Funding grant applications to FEMA for the City of Cheyenne (2019-2022 
1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $32,000.00). 
(#7276) 
 
   
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Little Joe Company, 
LLC, for the Janitorial Services for City of Cheyenne Facilities Project (General Fund, Fleet 
Maintenance Fund & Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and L and L, Inc. for the 
provision of golf services at the Airport Golf Clubhouse – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Lease Agreement  between the City of Cheyenne and L and L, Inc. for the use of the 
Kingham Prairie View Clubhouse and the provision of golf services at the Kingham Prairie View 
Golf Course – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County 
School District Number One (LCSD #1) to provide LCSD #1 use of city owned softball fields for 
practices and competitions – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and GLM Design Group 
for drainage engineering support (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract # 7206 between the City of Cheyenne and Inman 
Roofing, Inc., to replace wood nailer on Cheyenne Aquatic Center roof (General Fund -CIP 
Account).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $3,475.10). (#7206) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7257 between the City of Cheyenne and Clean Air 
and Water Systems, LLC, for the Happy Jack Landfill Phase 2, Cells 1 and 2 and Cell 3 Excavation 
Earthworks project for additional labor to install liner in the leachate trench (Solid Waste Fund) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7258 between the City of Cheyenne and Northwest 
Linings & Geotextile Products Inc., for the Happy Jack Landfill Phase 2, Cells 1 and 2 and Cell 3 
Excavation Earthworks project for additional liner and shipping cost for the leachate trench  (Solid 
Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract #6798 between the City of Cheyenne and Trihydro 
Corporation to extend the Environmental Monitoring at the Happy Jack Landfill for an additional 
three years (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-9-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and 4 Quarters 
Excavation & Development for the East Cheyenne Community Park -Parking Lot and Trailhead 
Construction Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried ( in an 
amount not to exceed $117,499.00). (#7277) 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
   
 Reappointment of Gregg Jones to the Brownfields Revolving Loan Committee.   Upon 
inquiry, it was advised Mr. Jones has served on the Brownfields Revolving Loan Committee for 
approximately 3 years.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did 
not vote (mayoral appointment).   
 
 Appointment of Don Pierson to the Police Civil Service Commission.  Mr. Segrave moved 
to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral appointment).  
 
 Under other business:  
  
 Mr. White noted the annual audit report by McGee, Hearne and Paiz, LLP was heard 
recently by members of the governing body and resulted in a clean audit with no recommendations.  
Mr. White expressed appreciation to the City Treasurer’s offices under the leadership of City 
Treasurer Robin Lockman.  Mayor Collins congratulated Ms. Lockman for the successful audit. 
 
 Mr. Johnson expressed condolences for the recent passing of prior city employee Scott 
Smith.  Mayor Collins also expressed condolences. 
 
 Mr. Laybourn reported on the ribbon cutting ceremony held at the Cheyenne A quatic 
Center for their reopening.  He also  commented on the Civic Center and how the Cheyenne  
Symphony Orchestra has been able to continue performing at this venue, noting however, the need 
for the facility to be renovated. Mayor  Collins expressed appreciation to Civic Center  staff for 
preparing the venue for various uses. 
 
  There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:29 p.m.  
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
  
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  January 31, 2021

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2021-02-08 | CLIP_ID: 788 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 788

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 8, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: 
MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich , Bryan Cook, Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 25, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of and adjacent to South Greeley Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch 
Road.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. (#4341) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential 
to AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial for land located east of and adjacent to South Greeley 
Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4342) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI (Light Industrial) to MR (Medium -Density 
Residential) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Pebrican Avenue and 
East 2nd Street.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Comments were made in support of the zone change and proposed development.  It was noted 
there have been multiple neighbor complaints concerning a neighboring property located at 609 
Pebrican which is currently without sewer and water.  Additional concerns were raised involving 
taking care of problem properties in neighborhoods so property values can be maintained.   Mr. 
Segrave declared a conflict of interest due to a business relationship and did not participate in the 
discussion.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
   
of Mr. Segrave who didn’t vote. (#4343)  
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne, changing the zoning classification from MR (Medium-Density Residential) to CB 
(Community Business) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Carbon 
Avenue and East 13th Street.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4344) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) Section 
5.8.4, Fences – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create the definition, ‘Family Unit’ and amend the definition of ‘Dwelling 
Unit’ – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.04, Streets, 
Sidewalks, and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Laybourn) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Bison 
Business Park, a plat of portions of Sections 28, 29, and 32, Township 13 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6 th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to  
South Greeley Highway between High Plains Road and Terry Ranch Road)  (Postponed from 
January 25, 2021).  Betsey Hale, CEO of Cheyenne Leads, expressed appreciation to the City for 
their involvement and work on the resolution to allow for the development of the business park.  
Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  White.  Dr. Rinne advised a motion to include 
Planning Commission Condition #1 was inadvertently left out at the Public Services Committee  
meeting, therefore, Dr. Rinne moved to amend by including the Planning Commission Condition 
#1, and revised final plat map dated February 2, 202 1, seconded by Mr. Cook.  It was confirmed 
by Dr. Rinne and Ms. Hale that  the substitute map changes the name of Bison Road to Loving 
Trail due to another road being named Bison Road west of town.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6122) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Office on 
Violence Against Women OVW-2021-37003 Improving Criminal Justice Responses to Domestic 
Violence, Dating Violence, Sexual Assault and Stalki ng Grant Program CFDA Number 16.590 -
Grants to Encourage Arrests Policies and Enforcement of Protection Orders Program .  Captain 
Nate Buseck, Acting Police Chief, reported that they are requesting this item not be approved and 
explained the grant is awarded annually and due to issues involving management of the grant, staff 
is recommending they visit approval next year.  He further advised the Police Department would 
like to work with the Sheriff ’s Department for future managem ent of the grant.   Captain Buseck 
noted managing the grant is almost a fulltime job in itself  and the funding pays for overtime for 
officers responding to domestic violence calls.  He feels the grant would be better managed by the 
   
County who can include other entities involved.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal 
advised the item died due to lack of a motion, therefore there is no recommendation.  Mayor 
Collins advised there has been positive communication relating to future partnerships for 
management of the grant.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones and City Attorney Michael 
O’Donnell provided a point of clarification that due to the lack of a motion, the item can die or the 
governing body can make a motion and second and vote it down.  Item died due to lack of motion.

 Appointments of Josh Chrysler and Amber Conwell to the Historic Preservation Board; 
and Richard Vallee to the Public Transit Advisory Board.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded 
by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral appointments).   
 
 Announcement was made that due to the President’s Day Holiday on Monday, February 
15, 2021, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held via electronic 
conference meetings as follows:  Finance Committee will meet Tuesday, February 16, 2021  at 
Noon and Public Services Committee will meet Wednesday, February, 17, 2021 Noon. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, February 22, 2021, at 
6:00 p.m. in the Cheyenne Civic Center, 510 W. 20 th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming,  and via 
electronic conference meeting , for renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, 
restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, winery 
and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.   City Clerk Kris Jones 
announced that due to a scheduling conflict, the February 22, 2021 meeting of the governing body 
will be held electronically only (via Zoom).  Mayor Collins explained the Cheyenne Symphony 
Orchestra previously reserved the facility on that date.   
 
 Under other business:  
  
 Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to C ivic Center and other City staff for their work 
in making it possible to host City Council meetings at the Civic Center. 
 
  There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:22 p.m.  
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2021-02-22 | CLIP_ID: 790 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 790

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:0 0 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Present were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins , 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete 
Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, 
City Clerk. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon request by Mr.  Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson, agenda items #16 & 17 were removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. White moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 8, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for r enewal applications for existing City liquor 
licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite 
distillery, winery and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  There 
being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, the applications were 
referred to Committee of the Whole  to be held on March 3, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. at the Cheyenne 
Civic Center and via electronic conference meeting, and Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
Section 5.8.4, Fences.  Brandon Swain voiced support for the ordinance.   Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  
Comment was made related to the proposed allowed 7’ height of privacy fences d iscouraging 
people from seeing over them to adjacent areas that may contain debris that needs to be picked up.  
It was also noted the rationale of increasing privacy fences from 6’ to 7’ is to align with what the 
International Building Code ( IBC) allows and that fences over 6’ require a building permit. Dr. 
Rinne moved to amend  Section 4(a)(2) by changing the fence height from  7’ to 6’, seconded by 
Mr. Cook.  Dr. Rinne noted the reason for the amendment would be to keep fence height the same 
with what it has been historically and because 7’ fences would require a building permit.  Upon 
inquiry, Charles Bloom, Planning and Development Director, advised that both 6’ and 7’ fences 
could be a visual barrier and it is u p to the governing body to determine what is aesthetically 
appropriate for neighborhoods.  He further noted that different fence heights ranging from 6’ -8’ 
aren’t uncommon in neighborhoods generally and that 7’ fence pickets are n’t available for 
   
purchase but 8’ pickets can be purchased and cut down.  Mr. Bloom further explained upon inquiry 
that the proposed setback from side streets is the main scope  of the ordinance.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “ yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Esquibel 
and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create the definition, ‘Family Unit’ and amend the definition of ‘Dwelling 
Unit’.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Concern was 
expressed related to ordinance timing due to a housing crisis in the community and there not being 
an efficient way to monitor or enforce the ordinance.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel and Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no”.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.04, Streets, 
Sidewalks, and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming . Amber Ash , Downtown Development Authority 
Director, advised the DDA board met last week and voted to support the ordinance.   She further 
explained she  has been working on th e ordinance with city staff for several months to find a 
solution to address downtown sidewalks without putting a burden on property owners.   It was 
noted that one concern is that if a property is located between 2 buildings, there can be issues with 
meeting DDA requirements.   Patti R iesland, Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities,  
commented on 2 studies conducted in the areas of 16th St. to 24th St. and from Warren to Pioneer 
that had results indicating an abundance of  disrepair between 16 th and 19th Streets.  She further 
noted that conditions are causing  difficulty for walking in certain areas  and there are  overall 
accessibility concerns resulting in negative economic impact as people with disabilities have issues 
with navigating aspects of  downtown streets, sidewalks and curbs .  Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to amend by substitute submitted February 16, 2021 by the attorney’s office, seconded by 
Mr. White.  Alessandra McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, explained the substitute includes 
clarification regarding responsibilities of the city and adjacent property owners noting the city is 
only responsible for curb and gutter and that language related to liens was also refined.  Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Support for the 
ordinance was expressed.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD -Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ordinance Number 3741, which established The 
Village Planned Unit Development (PUD), to allow mixed use business (MUB) District  Use on 
Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD located southwest of the intersection of Powderhouse Road 
and Old Town Lane – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 
 
   
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 5.68, Body Art Establishments and 
Operations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 5.12.150, Suspension or Revocation 
of License; Denial of Permit, of Chapter 5.12 , Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Cahill Park 
Addition, Filing No. 5, a replat of Lot 2, Block 1, Cahill Park Addition, Filing No. 4, Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Dell Range Boulevard between 
Windmill Road and Moran Avenue).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6123) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the sale of surplus city-owned property generally located at 
the northeast corner of Morrie Avenue and east Lincolnway, together with improvements thereon, 
and authorizing the Mayor to proceed with sale and transfer of the property.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried.  (#6124) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Calling for an Americans with Disabilities Act Compliance evaluation 
and transition plan for the City of Cheyenne Municipal Building .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6125) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing creation of a Community Technology Advisory Council and 
Directing the Mayor to appoint the same .  Gunnar Malm, County Commissioner, requested 
clarification on the composition of members of the advisory council.  Mayor Collins provided 
advisory council information explaining he is looking to bring a group of experts together to help 
him understand what is needed to enhance technology and promote a technology driven 
environment moving forward for businesses.  It was noted that the advisory council will help  
determine what is needed to make the city’s tech community stronger.  Mayor Collins further 
explained it is not anticipated that the advisory council will result in any financial impact.  Eric 
Trowbridge, CEO of Array, spoke in support of the resolution and advisory council and provided 
information on the broadband task force outlining 7 key recommendations to make sure the city is 
prepared for the future in technology.  Mr. Trowbridge advised it would be ideal that the advisory 
council deliver information to the governing body on an ongoing basis.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Discussion followed on possible language amendments to clarify the 
intent of the advisory council providing clarification on the member composition.  As sponsor of 
the resolution, Dr. Aldrich advised she was amenable to proposed amendments as the advisory 
council is non-binding and may include people that live outside of city limits and noted technology 
doesn’t stop at the city limits.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins provided additional information on 
the intent of the advisory council noting it will be a  group of individuals with knowledge in the 
field of technology providing advice for the future of technology.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend the 
5th “Whereas” paragraph by replacing “residents” with “members” and deleting the remainder of 
the paragraph, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Mr. Malm stated he is supportive of the amendment as it 
clarifies the membership and noted he  supports the advisory council.  Mr. White suggested a 
   
friendly amendment related to the motion on the floor r eplacing “residents” with “members” and 
deleting the remainder of the 8th paragraph.  Dr. Rinne accepted the proposed friendly amendment 
with Dr. Aldrich concurring as second to the motion.   (Amendment with friendly amendment: 
Paragraph 5, replace “residents” with “members” and remove the remainder of the sentence to 
read:  “Whereas, the Community Technology Advisory Council will be comprised of seven (7) 
members” and in the Be it further Resolv ed paragraph, replace “residents with “members” and 
remove “residents from the  City of Cheyenne, the County of Laramie and its incorporated towns 
and cities”). Motion to amend, including the friendly amendment, carried.   Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. (#6126) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing creation of an Innovation and Entrepreneur Advisory 
Council and Directing the Mayor to appoint the same .  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Aldrich explained this is a non -binding advisory council , there is a lot of 
entrepreneurial activity in the community and many other communities have this type of council.  
Dr. Aldrich further noted the Univer sity of Wyoming has received funding and will partner with 
LCCC to provide the opportunity to hear from people in many different fields about areas of 
interest that attract people and keep people in our community.   Dr. Rinne moved to amend by 
replacing “residents” with “members” and deleting the remainder of the paragraph for paragraphs 
6 and 9, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to amend carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  As a point of clarification, City Clerk Kris Jones noted agenda item #19 may have 
inadvertently not been requested to be removed from the agenda, and Mayor Collins explained the 
item can remain on the agenda. (#6127) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2021 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. 16 -4-112 & 16 -4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)

(#7290) 
 
[CA] Section 5307 COVID-19 Emergency Relief for Operations Grant Agreement between the

[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract # 7134 between the City of Cheyenne and  AVI

[CA] Contract Modification #1 for Renewal and Amendment of Agreement #7033 (Contract

[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -1-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Simon

[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-11-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and CXT, Inc. 
an LB Foster Company, Spokane, WA, for the Cheyenne Junior League Pre -Cast Restroom and 
Concession Building Project (Community Facility Fees and Recreation Mill Grant) – referred to

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-13-21 for furnishing eight (8) new four-door 2021 Model Utility

Cheyenne Police Department  (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-14-21 for furnishing eight (8) Motorola APX8500 Mobile Radios

 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 Appointment of Mark G. Francisco as Chief of Police for the City of Cheyenne.  Dr. Rinne 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral appointment). 
  
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, March 8, 2021, at 6:00 
p.m. in the Cheyenne Civic Center, 510 W. 20 th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic

for Machapuchare Inc. d/b/a Durbar Nepalese & Indian Bistro, 3515 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne,

 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, March 8, 2021, at 6:00

conference meeting , to determine whether compliance of annexat ion conditions required by 
Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for land located north of Dell Range Boulevard east of Griffith 
Avenue.   
 
 Under other business:  
  
 Amber Ash, Director of DDA, provided an update on the downtown  graffiti abatement 
program stating removal from buildings with the permission of property owners is in process.  She 
noted the DDA is also working with CoreCivic to apply paint that was provided by the Public 
Works Department to trash receptacles that have been ta gged.  It was further advised that Black 
Hills Energy has also been applying paint to graffiti on their boxes and David Hatch, owner of the 
Hynds Building, has whitewashed plywood on the Bell Building.  She noted that the DDA hopes 
to be able to install cameras in downtown areas to help curb graffiti activity in the future.  Ms. Ash 
expressed appreciation to Mark Christensen, Planning and Development, for presenting the West 
Edge Overlay District information to their board.  Mayor Collins thanked Ms. Ash for her efforts 
in supporting downtown.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Ash provided information on the mural program and 
explained generally the murals haven’t historically been tagged and that they provide vibrancy to 
downtown and help detour graffiti. 
 
 
   
 Mr. White moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-405(x),    
for the purpose of  a personnel matter related to Collective Labor Bargaining with Cheyenne 
Professional Fire Fighters, International Association of Fire Fighters, Local No. 279, seconded by 
Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mayor Collins 
announced members would meet in executive session v ia private conference call, not open to the 
public; requested the City Attorney, Deputy  City Attorney, City Clerk and City Treasurer be 
present and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive 
session, and that t he regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close of the 
executive session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 7:04 p.m.).     
 
 Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 8:03 p.m.   
 
  There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:03 p.m.

        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2021-03-08 | CLIP_ID: 793 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 793

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 8, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: 
MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich , Bryan Cook, Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 22, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 25, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on March 1, 2021 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s office for Machapuchare Inc. d/b/a Durbar Nepalese & Indian Bistro, 3515 E. 
Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met, Mayor Colli ns closed the hearing  and referred the application to Finance 
Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located north of Dell Range 
Boulevard west of Whitney Road.  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development, explained they 
received a petition to annex the land, provided information on the property  and reviewed 
requirements of compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Lloyd advised he would provide information relating to the p ercentage of boundary that is 
contiguous, noting this information was provided in the department’s staff report documentation.  
Rod Stone provided information about the engineeri ng firm on the proposed project stating they 
worked on the Thomas Heights project which has caused extensive flooding and damage to 
properties in the area.  Mr. Stone referenced a city funded  3rd party review in the amount of 
$26,000.00 which he doesn’t be lieve anything has been done with the report .  Dave Grigsby 
   
reported he bought his property in the  area of Thomas Heights 30 years ago and has experienc ed 
flooding from the project’s poor design.  Larry Bechtle advised h e also lives in th e area and has 
experienced flooding since August 2017 following the construction of the Thomas Heights 
addition and that the flooding has damaged his septic system .  Mr. Stone, Mr. Grigsby and Mr. 
Bechtle provided additional information relating to the development of Thomas Heights which has 
caused extensive damage and flooding the area and expressed frustration in trying to find resolve 
for those damages.  They stated the city may want to consider restricting the engineering firm from 
these types of developments until restitution and repairs have taken place.  They further provided 
comments relating to the Unified Development Code (UDC) which is supposed to ensure there is 
no damage to surrounding properties and referenced drainage issues in an area in Sun Valley that 
the same engineering firm designed.   Mayor Collins advised the city is working towards a solution 
that will hopefully be completed in the near future for the Thomas Heights area.  Mr. Grigsby also 
reported that City Engineer Thomas Cobb has been out to review the situation .  Information was 
requested for additional solutions and it was noted some property owners were advised by the city 
that one option would be to get a personal loan to repair property damages and issues.  It was noted 
that attempts to reach certain city officials regarding this issue have been challenging in the past.  
Discussion included concerns involving serious code violations, future developments with regards 
to damage to area prop erties from design flaws , determining who is responsible for repairing 
damage that may occur and future prevention efforts.  Additional comments were made relating to 
the 2019 3 rd party analysis receive by city , status of those results and lack of a solution for the 
problem.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, 
Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing  to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located north of Dell Range 
Boulevard east of Griffith Avenue.  Mark Christensen, Planning and Development, explained they 
received a petition to  annex the land, provided information on the property  and reviewed 
requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes .  There 
being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins 
closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
Section 5.8.4, Fences.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mr. Esquibel and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#4345) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create the definition, ‘Family Unit’ and amend the definition of ‘Dwelling 
Unit’.  Ursula Schultz spoke in favor of the ordinance.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Concern was expressed related to ordinance timing due to 
a housing crisis in the community and there not being an efficient way to monitor or enforce the 
ordinance.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#4346) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.04, Streets, 
   
Sidewalks, and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Amber Ash,  Downtown Development Authority  
Executive Director, spoke in support of the ordinance stating the design committee for the DDA 
will be considering an amendment for an improvement grant that would provide eligibility for 
funding under that program with the goal of combining what the city is responsible for with the 
DDA to help contribute to get sidewalks in good condition.  Reporting for Finance Committee, 
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to amend by substitute dated March 1 , 2021 , seconded by M r. White. Mayor Collins 
advised the substitute amends the amount and definition of curb and gutter.  Motion to amend by 
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   Concerns were voiced of 
deteriorating curb and gutter throughout the city.  Mayor Collins advised this issue can be reviewed  
through the Pavement Management Program.  Members of the governing body expressed 
appreciation to Mr. Laybourn for his continued efforts regarding failing concrete issues involving 
materials used and integrity of work being performed by contractors.   Support was voiced to the 
DDA for embracing options for solutions and it was noted that this project is a starting point for a 
limited area that can be evaluated and potentially implemented in other areas of town.  Discussion 
included potential for a 3 rd party to review materials used for concrete  and concerns with use of 
substandard materials and appreciation for looking at options for remediation and prevention of 
future concrete deterioration.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. (#4347) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD -Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ordinance Number 3741, which established The 
Village Planned Unit Deve lopment (PUD), to allow mixed use business (MUB) District Use on 
Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD located southwest of the intersection of Powderhouse Road 
and Old Town Lane .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve  on second reading , seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Chapter 5.68, Body Art Establishments and 
Operations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Alessandra McCoy Fakelman, 
Deputy City Attorney, prov ided a staff report stating this is a council initiated ordinance which 
includes mostly clerical language changes.  Ms. McCoy Fakelman reported a notable change is on 
page 2 to Section 5.68.020  which no longer exempts gun piercing of ear lobes  from licensing 
requirements and a change to the definition of language pertaining to minors receiving genitalia 
and breast area piercings and tattoos and noted she will also be proposing an amendment  to that 
section.  Steven Martinez, Bad Ass Tattoo Company, voiced concerns with the proposed changes.  
Mr. Martinez spoke to the longevity of businesses such as Claire ’s that provide ear piercing 
services using a gun piercing system.  He noted the proposed language would not allow for use of 
that system and would force patrons to seek more expensive services and spoke in opposition to 
the age restriction for this service.  He provided information relating to current city code and state 
statutes involving requirements for min ors including acceptable identification forms.  Mr. 
Martinez further stated he is unaware of any Health Department concerns with infection associated 
with these services.   He stated he is concerned  the proposed changes will push people to seek 
services out of town or they will result to home self -piercing methods.  Sativa Osiris, Diamond 
   
Dog Piercing, voiced support and reported concern with the repeated use of the term simple lobe 
piercing and noted that piercing the skin by any method is modifying the body.  Ms. Osiris advised 
the proposed regulations will allow such businesses as Claire’s to perform ear piercing services if 
they meet body art licensing regulations.  She noted the lack of sanitary conditions in stores such 
as Claire’s as they don’t currently have hand washing stations in their piercing areas and perform 
piercing services on carpeted surfaces .  Mr. Roybal advised the item failed at the Finance 
Committee, therefore there is no recommendation  from the committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by substitute 
dated March 8, 2021, seconded by Mr . Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Ms. McCoy Fakelman explained 
the proposed substitute clarifies the anatomical definition in Section 5.68.050( M) on page 7 by 
removing the word “ breast” replacing it with “ nipple”.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Upon inquiry, 
Mayor Collins explained the strike out of language pertaining to use of a piercing gun system 
simply no longer exempts  requirements of piercing.  Ms.  McCoy Fakelman confirmed  the 
proposed language removes the exception and removes the gun piercing from the definition in its 
entirety.  Upon inquiry, Dr. Aldrich reported a public forum was held and all current establishments 
holding body art licenses were notified and  there were no other comments  received other than 
those made by Mr. Martinez and Ms. Osiris.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. McCoy Fakelman advised 
that by removing the exception for  gun piercing systems, those types of piercing services will be 
required to follow current body art regulations.  Discussion included support for  unified health 
requirements and guidelines for body art services and support from the majority of the body artists 
in the community was received.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 5.12.150, Suspension or Revocation 
of License; Denial of Permit, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations, of the C ode of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming . Michael 
O’Donnell, City Attorney,  advised there is a recommendation to amend Section 5.12.150 (a)(2) 
referencing any violation relating to any state or local law, noting it will be modified to laws related 
to alcohol.  Upon inquiry from Bryan “Alf” Grzegorczyk, Alf’s Pub,  on the intent of the ordinance, 
Mr. O’Donnell advised the intent is to fill a void in current city code that only allows for suspension 
or revocation at renewal period and this will allow for consideration to suspend or revoke any time 
during the licensing year  upon violation of liquor laws.  Alf voiced support  and reported the 
majority of liquor license es follow the law s.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated March 8, 2021, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave.  [Clerk’s note:  Following adjournment of the meeting  and upon inquiry, it was 
determined the correct substitute date is March 5, 2021].  Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided 
additional information relating to violation of liquor laws and suspension and revocation process.  
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no”.
