MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2021-03-22 | CLIP_ID: 795 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 795

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 22, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: 
MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich , Bryan Cook, Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson,  Pete Laybourn,  Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave  (via 
Zoom) and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon request by Mr.  White, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Cook  and Mr. Roybal , agenda item s #21 and 26 (b) were removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Esquibel, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook 
and Dr. Aldrich, agenda items #28 (b) and 28 (d) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Esquibel moved to approve  remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Vot ing 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 8, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on March 10, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of retail liquor license application 
filed with the City Clerk’s office for Redwood Saloon, LLC d/b/a/ Redwood Lounge & Package 
Liquors, 2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Ace Majestic, Inc. 
d/b/a Redwood Lounge and Package Liquors).  There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing  and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD -Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ord inance Number 3741, which established The 
Village Planned Unit Development (PUD), to allow mixed use business (MUB) District Use on 
Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD located southwest of the intersection of Powderhouse Road 
and Old Town Lane.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4348) 
 
   
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 5.68, Body Art Establishments and 
Operations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Sativa Osiris, Diamond Dog Body 
Piercing Studio, commented on the concerns voiced relating to not allowing gun piercing .  Ms. 
Osiris reported the practice will still be allowed however, with the amendment, all businesses 
providing gun piercing will be required to adhere to body art establishment requirements.  Mr. 
Roybal advised the item failed at the Finance Committee due to lack of a second, therefore there 
is no recommendation from the committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final  
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Mr. Esquibel moved to amend by substitute to reinsert 
previously stricken language in Section 5.68.020 under “body art” and “body piercing” definitions 
that reinserts language providing an exemption for gun piercing, seconded by Mr. Johnson.   Ms. 
Osiris inquired as to the hesitancy for requiring these types of businesses to adhere to body art 
establishment requirements, noting skin is still being pierced and she doesn’t support the 
exemption. Ms. Osiris further  stated she believes big box stores providing gun piercing services 
should adhere to sanitary regulations as the small local businesses  do and reiterated these 
establishments work on carpet and do not have hand washing sinks.  Comments were made in 
support of health and safety concerns and o pposition to allowing preference to big box 
establishments.  Comments were voiced that there have been no health concerns reported by the 
Health Department regarding gun piercing by  local establishments or big box stores and it was 
noted big box stores hire local employees.  Additional comments were made regarding monitoring 
health concerns involving gun piercing at the State Health level.  Motion to amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn 
and Mr. White voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4349) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 5.12.150, Suspension or Revocation 
of License; Denial of Permit, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded b y Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4350) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 5.12.110, Open Container-Sales and 
Consumption-Exceptions, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne .  Alessandra 
McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, r eported on the ordinance changes for open container 
laws which reflect some of the changes that were allowed last summer.  Ms. McCoy Fakelman 
advised there are a few changes related to rodeo events that change the way that open containers 
align with statute.  She provided further information to changes to wine and champagne involving 
open container allowances and other resolution changes made last summer.  It was noted o pen 
containers within the downtown district boundaries  would be allowed between 8 a.m. and 10:00 
p.m., no glass will be allowed and only malt beverage products are allowed.  Ms. McCoy Fakelman 
further reported the Governing Body retains control by allowing for limitations that can be put in 
place by resolution if necessary.  She reported that certain holidays will always be allowed:  July 
4, Cheyenne Day, and Founders Day or Statehood Day.  Amber Ash, DDA  Executive Director, 
spoke in favor of the ordinance and noted she received positive feedback related to open container 
events last summer that were beneficial to businesses downtown.  She advised she would follow 
   
up after events with the Chief of Police, noting there weren’t many concerns with the exception of 
1 minor situation involving a specific individual.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB (Community Business) to MUB (Mixed -
Use Business Emphasis) for property generally located at the southwest corner of the intersection 
of McCann Ave.  and E. Pershing Blvd.   Christofer Lovas, AVI, agent for owner and applicant, 
advised the zone change is for Health Works which is a non -profit medical facility that provides 
care for under insured.  Mr. Lovas reported Health Works purchased 4 parcels and the zone change 
will allow them to move their facility closer to Pershing Blvd.  Mr. Lovas requested a 20’ setback 
instead of the 25’ setback that is currently required, noting there are some utility easements existing 
that will require this.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
Section 6.6.3.b.1, Encroachments.  Dr. Rinne moved t o approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located north of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Whitney Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Comments were voiced related to the large size of the 
annexation and it was noted there are concerns that need to be discussed relating to prevention of 
future drainage issues.   Discussion included the following topics:  prevention of drainage issues 
relating to the platting process, necessity of approving annexation and zoning requests when they 
have met criteria, noting the Governing Body has no legal justification to deny the annexation.  
Concerns were raised involving  annexing large parcels of land and  how the city becomes 
responsible for providing public safety and lack of planning to increase first responders, police, 
and fire personnel accordingly.  Additional comments were made relating to denial of annexation 
requests that meet the annexation criteria  and need for additional housing to accommodate 
community growth.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A -2 Agricultural to MR Medium -
Density Residential for land located north of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Whitney Road .  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted that this item 
is related to agenda item #14 and if the annexation is approved, the land must be zoned for the city.  
Motion carried.  Vo ting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. 
Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located north of Dell Range Boulevard and east of Griffith Avenue.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.   
   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MR (Medium -Density Residential) to 
MR (Medium-Density Residential) for property generally located north of Dell Range Boulevard 
and east of Griffith Avenue .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyoming 
Department of Transportation, Highway Safety Program, Grant Funding for the City’s Mobile 
D.U.I. Command Vehicle.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6130) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Establishing an Affordable Housing Task Force to assess the current and 
projected future landscape of housing affordability in the City of Cheyenne.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried).  (#6131) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Supporting and recommending the United States Congress propose and 
send to the states for ratification, an amendment to the United States Constitution clarifying the 
distinction between the rights of natural persons and the rights of corporations, unions, and other 
legal entities, and providing Congress and the State Legislatures the right to regulate monies raised 
and spent for political purposes.  Brenden McKinney, Wyoming Promise board member, advised 
the purpose of the resolution is to address and ratify through our constitution that people have the 
same rights as corporations when it comes to elections.  He reported  money in politics  isn’t 
primarily traceable and dark money has affected elections in Wyoming without the ability to track 
it.  He further no ted this is a b ipartisan issue and effort and commented on  issues related to 
elections in Georgia.  He stated the more this continues the less voice any of us will have in our 
elections and spoke in support of the resolution.  John Rivera, Wyoming Promise board member, 
stated he has been working on this issue for a number of years and had approached some council 
members last year , and expressed appreciation to Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook and Mr. Laybourn for 
their assistance.  Mr. River a provided detailed information on Wyoming Promise, a bipartisan 
organization, and a petition drive for the cause collecting over 25,000 signatures.  As many of the 
signatures were removed for various reasons, the petition was not submitted to the Secretary of 
State as it wasn’t eligible to be placed on the ballot due to Wyoming requirements.  He noted 22 
states have similar petitions and 34 states ar e required to get congress involved to propose an 
amendment to the constitution.  Mayor Collins requested speakers to stay on topic while speaking 
to the resolution.  Mr. Rivera stated t he purpose of the resolution is to support a change to the 
constitution, noting Congress can’t on its own propose resolutions.  Albert Simpson spoke in 
support of the resolution .  Boyd Wiggam spoke in opposition of the resolution voicing concern 
with the idea of the city lending support at an attempt to erode the first amendment by asking 
Congress to take up an amendment to curtail political speech, noting it is critical that people who 
have issues be able to have financial support to speak out.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Laybourn.  Comment was made that it is unusual for the Governing Body to bring the 
matter up for consideration since there was a determination by the supreme court that corporations 
are people and can donate to campaigns and that it can be deterioration of political process when 
races can be flooded by untraceable money.  Discussion included:  campaign regulations, concerns 
   
with untraceable funding, purpo se of those funds, c hallenges related to large amounts of money 
involved in Wyoming elections, purpose of resolution, 4th branch of government and separation of 
powers, visit from a Florida representative speaking in opposition of a Wyoming representative 
vote, timing of resolution involving the legislature, issue to make a difference at a local level as 
830 resolutions have been passed at local levels.  Comment was made that this type of legislation 
is inappropriate as the purpose of the Governing Body is to handle local issues .  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne 
voting “no”.  (#6132) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the issuance and sale by the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming of 
Revenue Refunding Bonds, Series 2021, in a principal amount not to exceed $5,445,000, for the 
purpose of refunding the City’s outstanding Refunding Revenue Bonds, Series 2012; prescribi ng 
the form of said Series 2021 Bonds; providing for the payment of the principal and interest thereon; 
ratifying, approving and confirming previous actions of officers and members of the Governing 
Body of the City and employees thereof; and determining ot her matters related to the bonds .  
Mayor Collins declared a conflict of interest involving a family member working at a bank related 
to the resolution and recused himself from discussion and voting.  Mayor Collins relinquished 
duties of chair to Council President White.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  
Mr. Roybal moved to amend to by adding Exhibit A which includes the final principal amount, 
interest rates and listing of the bond purchasers, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer, provided detailed information on the amendment and explained it is needed because 
bids weren’t received until March 8th.  Appreciation was voiced to Ms. Lockman and the Treasury 
staff for their efforts on the resolution.  Motion to amend carried. Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did  not vote.  Main motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor 
Collins who did not vote.  Following announcement of the vote, Mayor Collins resumed duties of 
the chair. (#6133)

 Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held April 12, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and  via electronic conference 
meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute 
§15-1-402 exist for land located northwest of the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and 
Whitney Road.  Mayor expressed appreciation to Civic Center staff, Michael Skinner, PIO Officer, 
and Jennifer McClelland, Executive Assistant to the City Council, for their assistance in u tilizing 
the Civic Center for City Council related meetings during the last 3 months.  Mayor Collins noted 
beginning April 12th, City Council meetings will resume in City Council Chambers.   
 
 Under other business:  
  
 Amber Ash, DDA Executive Director, requested consideration to provide comments 
relating to agenda item #19- Resolution Establishing an Affordable Housing Task Force.  Ms. Ash 
provided information involving a County Commission Impact Study related to  the impact of 
development and how to increase revenue.  She further noted there are challenges that come with 
annexations and that each time land is annexed  it ultimately expands the need for water, sewer 
police and fire services in those areas.  Ms. Ash reported there are areas currently available within 
the City with potential for infill development and in the downtown area there is more of an upfront 
cost for development. Mayor Collins confirmed her request to attend the task force meetings.   
 
 Dr. Aldrich expressed appreciation to first responders, Public Works staff, other city staff 
and contractors who provided tireless efforts during the recent historic blizzard.  It was noted that 
although there is always room for improvement, the first responders and city staff did a tremendous 
job.    
 
 Upon inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, confirmed agenda item #26 (b), which was removed 
from the consent agenda, was discussed and approved as amended by substitute.   
 
 Mayor Collins provided details relating to the recent historic blizzard and  expressed 
appreciation to city staff and the public and thanked them for their patience.  He extended the 
appreciation to contractors, first responders, and the County and WYDOT for their partnership in 
addressing the issues.  Mayor Collins acknowledge d that no matter how many assets you  may 
have, sometimes there are not enough locally when major weather events occur.   
   
 
  There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:21 p.m.  
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  March 31, 2021

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-04-12 | CLIP_ID: 797 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 797

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 12, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich , Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.   
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon request by Mr.  Segrave, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson, agenda item #21 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Cook, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave, agenda 
item #28(b) was removed from the Co nsent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve  remaining 
items, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing  
body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 22, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located northwest of the 
intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road. Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development 
Department, explained they received a petition to annex the land, provided information on the 
property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions contained in state 
statutes.  Mr. Lloyd explained agenda items #14 & #15 are affiliated with the annexation.  There 
being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins 
closed the hearing. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 5.12.110, Open Container-Sales and 
Consumption-Exceptions, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne.  Dominic 
Bravo, Visit Cheyenne President and CEO, spoke about the success of open container events held 
last summer, how the open container program will be an incentive for travel to Cheyenne and 
expressed support for the ordinance generally.  Tom H irsig, President and CEO  of Cheyenne 
Frontier Days, spoke in support of the ordinance from a community pe rspective related to 
downtown open container allowance and expressed concern with striking “malt beverage permits” 
in 5.12.110(B)(3) relating to activities held within Frontier Park.  Mayor Collins explained a 
substitute addressing the concern would be upcoming.  Muriah Kilmer, Freedom’s Edge, spoke in 
   
support of the ordinance stating she believes it promotes a safer drinking environment for people 
to walk around  with a drink  during events  downtown.  Lt.  David Janes, Cheyenne Police 
Department, provided information on calls for service during open container events last year and 
advised he believes the ordinance will assist in the public’s continued trust of the Police 
Department as there will not be a need for minor open container infractions within the parameters 
of the program.   Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to appr ove on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Johnson moved to amend by substitute  dated April 9, 
2021, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Alessandra McCoy Fak elman, Deputy City Attorney,  provided 
information on the proposed substitute noting malt beverag es are no longer stricken in 
5.12.110(B)(3) and that the bulk of changes occur for open containers in the DDA district.  Amber 
Ash, DDA Director, spoke in support of ordinance and provided information on open c ontainer 
events last year adding she believes this will make downtown Cheyenne a destination and will be 
positive for  the ability to onboard downtown events.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body . Upon inquiry, Mr. Johnson provid ed 
information on the open container hours, noting the intent is to include events downtown that may 
have early start times such as Frontier Days pancake breakfasts and parades.  Upon further inquiry, 
Mr. Johnson explained the inclusion of the entire DDA d istrict boundaries in the open container 
program is for possible future businesses that may locate downtown and also to allow 2 businesses 
located in south Cheyenne within the boundaries to participate.  Discussion continued and included 
comments related to concern for consumption in areas where daycare/preschool children may be, 
parking structures and other areas, alcohol consumption during pancake breakfasts and along the 
parade route, support of creating a district for open consumption, the large area co ntained in the 
DDA district boundaries and concern of lack of requirements for allowable plastic cups.  Mr. 
Johnson explained, upon inquiry, that public intoxication can occur regardless of whether or not 
cup size is limited and there is a financial burden on businesses if they are responsible for providing 
branded cups for events.  Further comments included: opinion that open container events from last 
summer didn’t provide a true “pilot program” for the proposed open container program downtown, 
concern wit h consumption in  certain areas in downtown , the large area of the DDA district 
boundaries consisting of  74 blocks, wide range of allowable consumption hours, importance of 
public education related to the ordinance changes and impact on City’s Clean and Safe Crew. Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  (#4351) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB (Community Business) to MUB (Mixed -
Use Business Emphasis) for property generally located at the southwest corner of the intersection 
of McCann Ave. and E. Pershing Blvd.  Chris Lovas, Lovas Engineering, agent for the applicant, 
advised he was available for any questions.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4352) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
Section 6.6.3.b.1, Encroachments .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final  reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by sub stitute dated March 25, 2021 , 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
   
the governing body.  Main  motion as amended  carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4353) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located north of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Whitney Road .  Joe Patterson, Whitney Ranch 
LLC, expressed appreciation to city staff and governing body.  Mr. Patterson reported he submitted 
the proper app lication, has met criteria as outlined in the staff report  and has been working 
diligently with staff on design , functionality and development impact fees .  He noted the 
development agreement is to address concerns for needs of city services in the area and hopes the 
governing body can expand on the document by shifting it to  a city-wide policy by allowing the 
city to recapture certain fees. Mayor  Collins expressed appreciation to Mr. Patterson for his 
willingness to agree to provide impact fees.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. White. Comment was made related to impact fees helping  offset costs and help 
make the annexation affordable and amenable to the community and  appreciation was expressed 
to city staff and Mr. Patterson for the development agreement for impact fees.  It was further noted 
the city has recently contracted with a drainage and storm water engineer to review drainage for 
the project.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4354) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A -2 Agricultural to MR Medium -
Density Residential for land located north of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Whitney Road.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on third and final  reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4355) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located north of Dell Range Boulevard and east of Griffith Avenue.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve

[CA] RESOLUTION – Directing Cheyenne Fire and Rescue to evaluate all facilities  and

(#6134) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Directing City Staff to initiate, develop, and execute a Property Assessed

 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a final plat for Whitney 
Ranch, 3rd Filing, a plat of a portion of the southwest quarter of Section 23, Township 14 North, 
Range 66 West, of the 6 th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of Dell 
Range Boulevard west of Whitney Road) .  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by M r. Segrave. 
Concern was expressed involving past unresolved drainage problems that were created by a past 
development.  Comment was made related to  a visit of the development site  with staff, the 
applicant and members of the governing body that assisted in providing a better understanding of 
the proposed development and drainage plan.  It was noted that for the subsequent development of 
the anticipated 4th filing of Whitney Ranch, it is important to make sure drainage off of the 
subdivision is done correctly.  Comments were made regarding importance of a future storm water 
facility and the City’s responsibility to review and make  drainage requirements.  Comment was 
made related to the city previously having a storm water engineer and support was expressed for 
hiring one in the future and for a storm water utility maintenance.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. (#6136) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Supporting the ‘Cruise the Legend’ event held in Cheyenne .  Dr. Rinne 
moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Members of the governing body expressed support  of 
the event , noted concerns with certain aspects of the event including noise disturbances and 
requested participants and organizers to practice good etiquette including consideration of 
residents living along the cruise route and to participate safely.  Mayor Collins expressed support 
for the resolution echoing the request for participants and organizers to  participate in a safe and 
considerate fashion.  It was reported there was representation from the event at the April 6, 2021 
Public Services C ommittee meeting and organizers are aware of  the need for info rmation to be 
dispersed for safe participation and that an organizer has worked with the Police Department .  
Comment was made that funds are being raised for non-profit organizations  during the event.   
(#6137) 
 
 
   
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Program Grant to provide the local 
match for operating funds for the City of Cheyenne for its Transit Program, as authorized by 4 9 
U.S.C.§5311 – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne’s Statement of Design Standards,

Cook and Mr. Esquibel) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and KLJ Engineering,

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $29,616.00). (#7319)

[CA] Development Agreement  between the City of Cheyenne and Whitney Ranch, LLC, a

Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Post & Associates,

of the governing body.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the

[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Western Research & 
Development, Ltd., Cheyenne, for design and construction management services of the Nationway

Center, Cheyenne, for occupational medicine – referred to Finance Committee.

Wyoming, LLC, Casper, WY, for the Capitol Basin – 26th Street Interceptor Storm Sewer 
Extension Project (2015-2018 and 2019 -2022 1 % Sales Tax).   Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (accept bid in an amount not to exceed $3,234,842.94). (#7325)

Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department (Federal Grants and General Fund) – referred to Finance

Fence Co. Inc., Cheyenne, for the fencing and gates for the City of Cheyenne Compost Facility 
Project (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.

cold mix on an annual basis, for the City of Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division (2019-2022 1 % 
Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.

of Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division (2019-2022 1 % Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Transfer of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Redwood 
Saloon, LLC d/b/a/ Redwood Lounge & Package Liquors, 2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (license currently held by Ace Majestic, Inc. d/b/a Redwood Lounge and Package 
Liquors).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 City of Cheyenne Naming and Memorial Policy .  Mr. Cook advised it was brought to his 
attention following the April 6, 2021 Public Services Committee meeting that this item will need 
to be formally adopted by a resolution.  It was recommended that a motion to acknowledge receipt 
be used instead  of the motion to adopt for this particular agenda item  which was used in the 
committee meeting.  Mr. Coo k explained that a resolution to formally adopt the proposed policy 
is on the agenda for referral to the Public Services Committee.  Mr. Cook moved to acknowledge 
receipt, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Michael O’Donnell confirmed the 
suggested motion to acknowledge receipt is appropriate  for th e agenda item.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.   
 
   
 Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held April 26, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action Plan Fiscal Year 2021, prepared by the 
Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office. 
 
 Notice of Emergency Procurement.  In accordance with the City of Cheyenne Purchasing 
Policy, an Emergency Procurement has been authorized by Mayor Collins for the emergency 
replacement of the damaged traffic signal at the intersection of East Pershing Boulevard & Logan 
Avenue.  The emergency repairs have been authorized to Casper Electric for $73,250.00.  
 
 Under other business: 
 
 City Clerk Kris Jones advised the following item was being introduced and referred to 
Committee of the Whole to be held on April 21, 2021 in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue:  Resolution setting the City’s priorities for their allocation on the 1% Specific Purpose 
Sales and Use Tax in Laramie County, State of Wyoming, that would be put together by the County 
Attorney for a Special Election to be held on November 2, 2021. (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave) 
 
 Dr. Aldrich provided information on a public forum to be held on April 15,  2021 from 6-
7:30 p.m. at the Kiwanis Community House related to proposed city code regarding mobile storage 
units.  Dr. Aldrich further expressed appreciation to  BOPU staff for a recent  tour of the water 
treatment facility. 
 
 Mr. Johnson requested ceasing the use of the phrase “needs vs. wants” relating to education 
and discussion on 6th Penny Ballot projects. 
 
 Mr. White and Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to City staff who have assisted with 
the development of the plan for City Council to reconvene in council chambers after 13 months.  
 
  There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:12 p.m.  
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-04-26 | CLIP_ID: 799 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 799

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 26, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich , Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.   
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. White, agenda item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Segrave and Mr. White, 
agenda item #23(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve  
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 12, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action Plan 
Fiscal Year 2021, prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office .  
Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development, provided a brief overview of the history 
and purpose of the Community Development Block Grant program , noting the proposed plan is 
consistent with the 5 -year Comprehensive Consolidated Plan  completed in 2020, and stated the 
anticipated allocation funding amount is $478,351.00 and $127,319.00 in recaptured funding.  Ms. 
Widauf reviewed information on the grant application process ; public notifications and 
opportunities for input; role of the Advisory Council in reviewing and recommending projects to 
be funded and explained the projects that are recommended for funding .  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee , Dr. Rinne  moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City o f 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-2 Agricultural to AG Agricultural for 
land located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney 
   
Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded b y Mr. White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Rescinding Footnote 1 in Ordinance No. 4314 
appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting 
the municipal government of said city and fixing the amount of General and Special Taxes as part 
of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the fiscal year beginning July 1, 
2020, and ending June 30, 2021 .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Michael O’Donnell explained rescinding footnote 1  
eliminates the cap on employees  and noted there is no substitute  document. Mayor Collins 
explained upon inquiry that there will be a  large upcoming development project, and there is a 
need to hire a plan reviewer and tw o inspectors to handle the project.  Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer, provided information on the revenue amount the city will receive in relation to  the 
project.  Mayor Collins confirmed the footnote would expire at the end of the current fiscal year 
and that if the ordinance is approved, the footnote will be removed for the remaining months of 
this fiscal year.  Mayor Collins further expressed the need to recruit and hire necessary employees 
prior to June 30, 2021.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
increase the total lot coverage of the MD1, MD2, P -CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types to 80% -- 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Creating Chapter 8.29, Hood System Cleaning 
Companies, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Program Grant to provide the local 
match for operating funds for the City of Cheyenne for its Transit Program, as authorized by 49 
U.S.C.§5311.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6138) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne’s Statement of Design Standards, 
Naming, Memorial and Gifting Policy .  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Comment was made on the standards, page 2, under “Naming or Renaming for Exceptional 
Individuals”, 3rd and 4th lines pertaining to naming a facility after a person is deceased and it was 
noted the city hasn’t followed that practice  in the past. Dr. Rinne moved to amend the standards 
   
by deleting the sentences relating to naming rights after a person is deceased , seconded by Mr. 
Johnson. Teresa Moore, Director of Community Recreation and Events, upon inquiry, advised the 
naming standard used by their department used to require that 7 years must pass after a person had 
died prior to facility naming consideration and that at the request of Mr. Cook and Mr. Esquibel, 
it was reduced to 2 years.  It was noted th is document was crafted 10 years ago by the Parks & 
Recreation Department and  was a common component based on past situations where a facility 
had to be renamed and was designed to address certain situations that can be handled differently.  
Comment was made that other agencies in the community limit naming rights to people who are 
deceased.  Mr. O’Donnell confirmed upon inquiry that this proposed policy serves as a guide but 
allows for flexibility.  Comments supporting the amendment were voiced.  Motion to  amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Roybal and 
Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  It was advised that a request was made to the Historical Preservation 
Board to research and provide history on naming rights, noting the rich history the Cheyenne 
community has.  Comment was made that city owned facilities should not have a person’s name 
affiliated with it  and that government entities have different policies relating to naming rights.  
Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#6139) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Setting the City’s priorities for their allocation on the 1% Specific 
Purpose Sales and Use Tax in Laramie County, State of Wyoming, that would be put together by 
the County Attorney for a Special Election to be held on November 2, 2021.   Amber Ash, 
Downtown Development Authority (DDA) Director, expressed appreciation to the governing body 
for their consideration for downtown district improvements noting many downtown infrastructure 
elements have deteriorated.  Reporting for Committee of th e Whole, Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated April 22, 2021 to 
include the following amendments made at the April 21 st Committee of the Whole meeting:  
Revised entitlement language; removing item  #5-Police Ammunition Purchase Project in the 
amount of $40,000.00 and moving the funding in the amount $40,000.00 to item #13 -Downtown 
Development Authority (DDA) District Improvements and Maintenance Project for a total of 
$6,017,966.00 and renumbering r emaining sections . The substitute also includes the following 
amendments that were discussed but not amended during the meeting:  Language revisions to item 
#8-Municipal Building Space Need Analysis Plan Project to reflect inclusion of design and 
engineering elements and creation of a fund for  other needs at the current municipal building if 
funds are available ; language revisions to item #7 -Johnson Pool Renovation  Project to reflect 
inclusion of design and engineering elements and determination of re novation or replacement 
costs, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted that the road maintenance project and airport parking 
lot pavement project were  discussed at the April 14 th Committee of the Whole meeting  as 
standalone projects on the ballot.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  County Commissioner Gunnar Malm, upon request, provided information on the 
upcoming April 28th meeting and explained it would be on Zoom and open to the public for limited 
number of attendees, noting members of the 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax C ommittee 
will also be in attendance to start going through the projects.  Mr. Malm explained there will be 3 
bundle types for the projects and they will be put together in likeliness of projects and that a draft 
of projects would be presented at the meeting for consideration and discussion by the committee.  
He further noted there will be consideration for the City and Laramie County for standalone road 
   
projects and that some Laramie County fire projects could be paired with the City’s fire projects.  
Commissioner Malm, upon inquiry, advised that although the state statute has been revised to 
decrease the number of rural communities needed to approve the projects, the goal is for all towns 
to support approval.  He reported the committee wants to be as inclusive as possible and work as 
a team as the county  consists of 100,000 people.  Comment was made in support of incl usion of 
all affected community members.  Main motion as amended carried.   Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#6140) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne Annual Action Plan 2021, prepared by 
the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Segrave) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board in the submission of 
an application for federal assistance to the Federal Aviation Administration under the Coronavirus 
Response and Relief Supplemental Appropriations Act of 2021 to assist in funding costs related 
to operations, cleaning, sanitization, janitorial services, combating the spread of pathogens at the 
airport, and debt service payments – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board in the submission of 
an application for federal assistance to the Federal Aviation Administration for ‘Des ign and

Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Directing City staff to plan and execute the completion of the 17th Street 
Lighting Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Segrave) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Recognizing the historical interest and value of the Cheyenne Auxiliary 
Pumping Station located at 1504 Dillon Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and directing the Cit y of 
Cheyenne to proceed with the steps necessary to protect and enhance this historic structure – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn)  
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Stitches Acute Care 
Center, Cheyenne, for occupational medicine.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount 
not to exceed $11,000.00). (#7331) 
 
CA] Cable Franchise Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Clarity 
Telecom, LLC – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment #1 to City Contract #7109 between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming 
Office of Homeland Security extending the term of the grant for the Cheyenne Regional Response 
Team #7 through December 31, 2021 (EMW-2019-SS-00065 Grant) .  Motion (per Consent

 Renewal of Contract #6837 between the City of Cheyenne and Delta Dental for group 
dental insurance benefits for City employees .  Mr. Johnson declared conflict of interest due to a 
work affiliation and vacated council chambers .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Dr. 
Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Johnson who didn’t vote. (#6837)  
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -19-21 for furnishing concealable bo dy armor for the City of

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -20-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Alco

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -25-21 for furnishing bituminous asphalt hot mix, tack oil, and

[CA] Consideration of Bi d #S-15-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Craig 
Construction Services LLC, Laramie, WY, for the Michael Drive Sidewalk Project (2019-2022

[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -16-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Craig

[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-17-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Streamline

referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -21-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2020 -2021 front end wheel 
loader mounted, two stage blower for the City of Cheyenne, Street and Alley Division (2019-2022

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-22-21 for furnishing one (1) ¾ ton crew cab truck for the Traffic

and three (3) ¾ ton crew cab trucks  for the City of Cheyenne, Street and Alley Division (2015-

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -23-21 for furnishing two (2) new 2020 to 2021 mid -size SUVs

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -24-21 for furnishing one (1) 2021 -2022 mid-size pick-up truck

– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -27-21 for furnishing four (4) new Kubota RTV -X1100C utility 
vehicles for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events Department (2015-2018 1% 
Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Appointment of Stefanie Boster to the Planning Commission .  Mr.  Segrave moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Appreciation was expressed to Ms. Boster for being willing 
to serve.  Upon inquiry, Mr.  O’Donnell provided information on Ms. Boster’s background and 
how it relates to the Planning Commission.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the

 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-05-10 | CLIP_ID: 800 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 800

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 10, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel (via 
Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon re quest by  Dr. Rinne , and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. White, agenda item #32 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 26, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located north of Pershing 
Boulevard and west of Christensen Road.   Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, 
explained they received a petition to annex the land, provided information on the property  and 
reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  Mr. 
Lloyd explained agenda items #21 & # 22 are affiliated with the annexation  and s ingle-family 
homes and duplexes are proposed for the area.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lloyd explained the intersection 
of Pershing & Christensen is currently outside of city limits.  There being no further comments or 
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located east of and adjacent to 
South Greeley Highway [US 85], north of Chalk Bluff Road. Seth Lloyd, Planni ng and 
Development Department, explained they received a petition to annex the land, provided 
information on the property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd explained agenda items #23 & #24 are affiliated with the 
annexation and advised the land is proposed to be used for industrial purposes.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Lloyd explained there are approximately 432 acres that will be annexed between the prior public 
hearing annexation and this one.  Concern was expressed with expanding emergency services to 
annexed areas.  Mr. Lloyd advised Police Department and Fire Department  are asked to review 
annexations and plats and he doesn’t believe there were any comments made, noting however, the 
   
absence of comments doesn’t mean there won’t be any concerns with expanding services.  
Comment was stressed that the city needs to make sure services can be covered  when new areas 
are annexed.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located southwest of the 
intersection of Br aehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive. Seth Lloyd , Planning and 
Development Department,  explained they received a petition to annex the land, provided 
information on the property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd explained agenda items #25 & #26 are affiliated with the 
annexation and duplex development is proposed for the area. There being no further comments or 
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a bar and grill liquor license application filed 
with the City Clerk’s Office for HRARG DH Cheyenne, WY, LLC, d/b/a Dog Haus, 3838 Atkin 
St., Cheyenne Wyoming.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, upon inquiry, confirmed the current owners hold 
a bar and grill liquor license and advised bar and grill liquor licenses are not transferable and this 
request is due to change in ownership.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Tres Amigos Incorporated d/b /a Tres Amigos Family Mexican 
Restaurant, 2414 W. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the 
application to Finance Committee.

 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to

 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Rescinding Footnote 1 in Ordinance No. 4314 
appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting 
the municipal government of said city and fixing the amount of General and Special Taxes as part 
of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for t he fiscal year beginning July 1, 
2020, and ending June 30, 2021 .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final  reading, 
seconded by Mr.  White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#4361) 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, explained this is the annual amendment of 
the fee schedule and provided information on the areas of increase.  Ms. Nemecek reported that 
residential trash and recycling services are subsidizing other services.  She provided details on 
proposed changes which include: removal of 1.5 yard waste containers, removal of acceptance of 
single items at transfer station, increased fee for 1.5 cubic yard container cleaning, increased fee 
for disposition of tires based on weight, and removal of “There is no guarantee the optional yard 
waste collection program will be available upon return” sentence in Note 11.   Ms. Nemecek 
advised that upon request from members of the governing body,  she will work on a media 
campaign for informational purposes for the solid waste program.  Ms. Nemecek provided 
clarification on removal of 1.5 cubic yard waste containers and the increase in cleaning fee of the 
1.5 cubic yard container.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised over the years they have 
been looking at whether containers are picked up on the street or in an alley and explained that 
pick up location is consistent per  block and/or neighborhood .  Ms. Nemecek exp lained, u pon 
inquiry, that there was a previous increase for the yard waste program of $1.20 so they could invest 
in more equipment , drivers and bins so the program could be expanded, and bins are available . 
She further explained since the program fee increase, money is not being lost on the program, but 
services aren’t being used efficiently as there aren’t enough subscribers.  Additional drivers and 
equipment are being utilized in other areas of the solid waste program for additional pick-ups, etc. 
During discussion related to Colorado taking plastic numbers 1-6, it was noted the expectation is 
plastics recycling overall will decrease, not increase.  Appreciation was expressed to Ms. Nemecek 
for working on the PSA regarding recycling.  Ms. Nemecek provided information on the “Recycle 
Coach” app which can assist customers.  Upon further inquiry related to why plastic recycling isn’t 
expanding, Ms. Nemecek advised  contractors typically are not willing to take a loss due to 
accepting materials they will lo se money on such as plastics .  Ms. Nemecek noted tin cans, 
cardboard and glass provide a positive return, however, to accept glass, they would have to increase 
program fees.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.   Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
increase the total lot coverage of the MD1, MD2, P -CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types to 80% .    
Dr. Rinne advised there was no recommendation on the item at the Public Services Committee .  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Dr. Rinne reported 
concerns were raised during the Public Services Committee that although the proposal is to allow 
greater building coverage in those lot types, it would leave a negative effect relating to decreased 
landscaping requirements.  Comment was made that caution was raised by the Board of Public 
Utilities relating to water usage concerns involving landscaping water requirements.  Additional 
   
concerns were raised in areas relating to landscaping, primarily trees, which have died due to lack 
of maintenance in areas such as around the airport terminal.  Comments were made that developers 
follow landscaping requirements when  properties are developed, but those green areas  are not 
maintained after completion.  Concerns were raised  related to management and enforcement of 
landscaping requirements and request was made to take this o pportunity to look at  the current 
landscaping code for possible revisions.   Mayor Collins advised staff is currently working with 
property owners on possible improvements.  It was further advised  each development will be  
required to resize runoff facilities to accommodate terrain changes involving landscaping.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. 
Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating Chapter 8.29, Hood System Cleaning 
Companies, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Division 
Chief Byron Mathews, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department, reported the  creation of  the 
ordinance is for hood cleaning contractors to ensure that those contractors who clean hood systems 
are properly licensed, insured and trained.  Mr. Mathews advised this ordinance m irrors the fire 
extinguisher business licensing requirement.  Mr. Mathews, upon inquiry, provided information 
on a recent incident where an individual who did not have proper training provided services to a 
local restaurant  and the restaurant equipment was left at high risk for catching  fire and food 
contamination.  Mr. Matthews explained that  with approval of the ordinance, the fire department 
will know who is providing service s and can make sure they are qualified. He reported last year 
there were approximately 10 instances where hood  systems were  not properly maintained or 
cleaned.  It was noted that the ordinance was drafted at the request and in consultation with local 
HVAC and knowledgeable hood cleaning contractors.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-05-24 | CLIP_ID: 801 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 801

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 24, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, 
Pete Laybourn,  Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave  and Jeff White.  Absent: Ken 
Esquibel.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 EXPRESSION OF APPRECIATION TO THE 2020-2021 MAYOR’S YOUTH 
COUNCIL AND STATE OF THE YOUTH ADDRESS. Jan Spires, Youth Alternatives and staff 
advisor to the Mayor’s Yout h Council, expressed appreciation to Youth Council members, 
recognized their year in service and introduced Emily Lucero , Chair, for the Mayor’s Youth 
Council, who presented the State of the Youth Address.  Ms. Lucero  reviewed projects and 
programs the group participated in throughout the year.  She expressed appreciation to Ms. Spires, 
Michael Skinner , City Public Information Offi cer, Ha ylee Chenchar and Amber Ash with the 
Downtown Development Authority , Mayor Collins and fellow Youth Council members.  Ms. 
Spires thanked the member’s parents for allowing them to participate  with the council and  
introduced and presented awards to th e following members: Kaylee Ashley, David Barker, Ty 
Bronder, Garrison Cook, Makiya Green, Nevaeh Green, Emily Lucero, Zoey Lundin and Elizabeth 
Stump.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda ).  Mr. Johnson declared a conflict of interest due to an 
employment affiliation and did not vote.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mr. Johnson who did not vote. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 10, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a retail liquor license transfer of location 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Poor Richard’s, from 2233 E. Lincolnway to 
1808 O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Inquiry was made related to the location and scope of 
the business and it was noted no representatives were present to report on the inquiry.  There being 
no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed 
the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
   
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a retail liquor license  transfer of ownership 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for ARCHCO Albany, LLC d/b/a Albany Restaurant, 
Bar, and Package Li quor, 1506 Capitol Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by 
Albany, Inc. d/b/a Albany Restaurant, Bar and Liquormart).  Brian Bau, ARCHCO Albany, LLC 
representative, advised he was available to answer any questions.   There being no  further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing 
and referred to application to Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a bar and grill liquor license application filed 
with the City Cler k’s Office for Capitol Cuisine LLC  d/b/a Ike’s Bar & Grill , 122 West 6 th St., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. Upon inquiry, Brian Eicholtz, Ike’s Bar & Grill, provided information on 
the extensive remodel of the building and advised the business will be a neighborhood bar and 
grill.  There being no  further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, 
Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, advised she was available for any questions.  
Reporting for Finance Committee,  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final  reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#4361) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
increase the total lot coverage of the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types to 80% .    
Dr. Rinne advised there was no recommendation on the item at the Public Services Committee .  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Comments 
were made regarding continue d lack of maintenance of landscaping following planting resulting 
in death of plants/trees which are not being replaced.  Discussion included the need to review 
landscaping requirements and maintenance responsibilities.  It was noted that the proposed 
amendment only effects certain lot types  and is a way to save water usage .  Concern was raised 
regarding reduced area of green in some areas to 50’ x 50’, noting landscaping is important for 
various reasons.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend all references to 80%  be changed to 70%, seconded 
by Dr. Aldrich.  Dr. Rinne noted the proposed amendment is an attempt to compromise, and his 
preference would be to revisit in a year.  It was reported that some lots currently have less than the 
70% required.  Motion to amend failed. Voting “yes” - Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and 
Mr. White.  Voting “no” - Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave. 
Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with exception 
of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#4362)  
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating Chapter 8.29, Hood System Cleaning 
Companies, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Upon inquiry, 
Division Chief Byron Mathews, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue Department, clarified that businesses 
will be required to be licensed moving forward with no provisions to grandfather businesses.  Chief 
Mathews explained that although there may be an initial impact on businesses due to uniformity 
and initial higher cost for cleaning , eventually there would be a cost savings  which will result in 
   
an overall safety for consumers moving forward.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present. (#4363) 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 5.12.030, Licenses and Permits -
Types, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr . Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . 
White. It was noted that initially it was believed  delivery services may reduce DUI’s, however, 
after the li mited 6-month period during the COVID pandemic, they actually increased.  Further  
comments included: support for the ordinance, limited time frame and increased alcohol intake 
due to the COVID pandemic may have contributed to the increase of DUI’s, more establishments 
will likely participate if delivery is available permanently and the importance of businesses being 
able to compete and provide additional services.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting no. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming Mr. Roy bal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Repealing Chapter 2.52, Personnel Commission, and 
creating a new Chapter 2.52, Personnel System, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department and 
CPEA representative, noted concerns relating to this and the following agenda items pertaining to 
proposed changes to the Personnel Commission.  Mr. Lloyd reported d issolving the commission 
is concerning to city employees  and requested clarification on the effect this will have on 
employees, urging caution proceeding with the proposed changes.  Linda B urt, Personnel 
Commission Vice Chair , voiced concerns and explained members of the commission are 
volunteers and the commission  provides fairness and due process .  Ms. Burt noted  the proposal 
calls for the hearing officer to be paid for by the city which may give the appearance of bias.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.   Dr. Rinne moved to amend 
by substitute dated May 24, 2021, seconded by Mr.  White.  City Attorney Mike O’Donnell 
explained the amendment clarifies that the existing grievance procedure  will not be eliminated .  
Mayor Collins overruled a point of order raised regarding comments not being germane to the 
agenda item on the floor.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised concerns were raised on removing 
the opportunity of having a jury of peers participate in hearing procedures for city employees.  Mr. 
Lloyd reported there is an impression by city employees that the commission is a trial by jury and 
if the city appoints a hearing officer, that person may be predisposed to rule in favor of the city.  It 
was additionally noted employees fear the new procedure won’t be as fair as the current process. 
Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided information on the current Personnel Commission which 
   
consists of 7 members - 3 city employee representatives and 4 citizens appointed by the Mayor.  
Mr. O’Donnell explained that due to the volunteer status of members, it can be difficult to assemble 
the commission for hearings.  Motion to amend carried.  Comments followed relating to support 
for the ordinance, informal grievance process,  concerns involving one individual to serve as 
hearing officer, establishing a fair process, inclusion of a neutral party hearing officer, providing 
training to Personnel Commission members and potential of a conflict of interest when employee 
representative members of the commission are employees of the same department the petitioner is 
employed by.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Repealing Chapter 2.92, Personnel System, and creating 
a new Chapter 2.92, Administrative Hearing Procedure, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, 
of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr . Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present.   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Granting a non -exclusive franchise to Visionary 
Communications, Inc., within the rights -of-way of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Stacy 
McDonald, Visionary Broadband, expressed appreciation and explained the business in the 
community and state.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
align the UDC fee schedule with the fee adoption practices of the City .  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located north of Pershing Boulevard, west of Christensen Road .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential 
to MR Medium -Density Residential for land located north of Pershing Boulevard, west of 
Christensen Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of and including South Greeley Highway (US 85), north of Chalk Bluff Road .  Dr.
