City Engineer, advised he has been in contact with the state regarding the proposal to annex South 
Greeley Highway and anticipates receiving a response this week.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present.   
   
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A -2 Agricultural to AG Agricultural, 
LI Light Industrial, and HI Heavy Industrial for land located east of South Greeley Highway (US 
85), north of Chalk Bluff Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive .  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-Density Residential for land located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, 
Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap a nd system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities .  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . White.  Comments were made 
acknowledging that fee increases aren’t generally favored and that BOPU did an excellent job of 
looking at surrounding communities’ fee structures as references for the fee adjustments.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue re quired to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2021 and ending June 30, 2022.  Mr. White 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. White moved to amend by 
substitute dated May 20, 2021 including the following amendments: increasing the City Attorney 
Division by $123,200.00 and decreasing Miscellaneous Division by the same amount; increasing 
the Clean and Safe Division by $73,000.00 and decreasing the Miscellaneous Division by the same 
amount and increasing the Compliance Division by $18,278.00 and increase revenue from reserves 
in the same amount, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to divide the question to discuss 
and vote on the amendments separately, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present with the exception of  Mr. Roybal and Mr. White 
voting “no”.  Item #1-motion to approve increasing the City Attorney Division by $123,200.00 
and decreasing Miscellaneous Division by the same amount carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present.  Item #2-increasing the Clean and Safe Division by $73,000.00 and 
decreasing the Miscellaneous Division by the same and amount .  Discussion included:  Use of 
one-time funds for on-going expenses such as permanent full -time employees, options for other 
funding sources that may be available for Depot Plaza repairs and maintenance, and efforts to fund 
positions from the general fund rather than one-time funding.  Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer, advised depending on sales tax collections, it could be possible to use some 
   
discretionary funds to aid in moving employees under the general fund.  Comments were made 
regarding sales tax funding for the Botanic Gardens operations and maintenance which is currently 
be utilized for a few employees’ salaries and concerns relating to depleting that funding.  Support 
was voiced for moving full -time staff under the general fund instead of temporary tax funding.  
Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins clarified the proposed funding could be allocated to other areas of 
the budget if the motion fails.  Additional discussion includ ed the importance of repairing and 
maintaining the Depot Plaza for tourism and image, noting improvements will help generate sales 
tax and other revenue.  Comments were made on the value of operations and maintenance funding, 
functions of the Clean & Safe Division, and lack of available funding to move Botanic Gardens 
staff under the general fund.   Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised a vote in favor of the motion 
before the governing body would move the funding into the Clean & Safe Division.  Ms. Lockman, 
upon inquiry, reported the funding in question would only take care of the salary for one position, 
noting there is still significant amount of benefits to be covered.  Jason Sanchez , Community 
Recreation & Events Deputy Director, explained a recent site visit was done at the Depot Plaza 
and several areas for repair and maintenance were identified and that funds from this amendment 
would be used for that purpose.  Mr. Sanchez  reviewed Depot Plaza areas that need maintained 
and noted the goal is to restore  the plaza.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White voting “no”.  Item 
3-increasing the Compliance Division by $18,278.00 and increase revenue from reserves in the 
same amount. Comments were made regarding a recent Ward I Town Hall public meeting which 
highlighted nuisance issues.  Supportive comments were voiced noting the importance of assisting 
in the efforts.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   Main 
motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of t he governing body  
present.  The following is a summary of the proposed budget , including the aforementioned 
amendments: General Fund, $53,795,619; Weed and Pest, $ 573,274; Youth Alternatives Grants, 
$882,886; Recreation Programs, $1 ,477,947; Community Development Block Grant, $ 617,074; 
Law Enforcement Grants, $ 252,616; Transportation Planning, $ 866,101; Transit, $2,603,121; 
Juvenile Justice, $ 67,640; Special Friends, $ 84,664; Development Impact Fees, $ 212,800; One 
Percent Sales Tax 2019 -22, $11,087,381; Speci fic Purpose Option Tax,  $601,905; Youth 
Activities/Ropes, $18,221; Golf Facilities, $276,590; Fleet Maintenance, $4,460,139; Solid Waste 
Management, $19,301,898; Civic Center, $ 2,057,777; Ice & Events Center, $828,665; Perpetual 
Care, $ 3,000; Total City, $ 100,069,318; Water Enterprise, $ 23,619,580; Sewer Enterprise, 
$14,123,800; Total Appropriation, $137,812,698.

 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Creating Section 13.04.141, Fee Assessment on Gross

the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyo ming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr.

 RESOLUTION – Rescinding Resolution No. 6023 which establishes that all revenue from 
City Contract No. 5902 be split evenly between the City of Cheyenne Solid Waste Enterprise Fund, 
the Board of Public Utilities, and the newly established Belvoir Ranch Recreation Special Revenue 
Fund.  Cari Cuffney spoke in favor of the anticipated substitute to be introduced and noted the 
contract provides ongoing maintenance funds for the Belvoir Ranch masterplan development.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated 
May 17, 2021, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. O’Donnell provided information on the substitute 
stating the original proposed resolution redirected all money to the general fund and the proposed 
substitute amendment directs 2/3 of the funding to the general fund and 1/3 to the Belvoir Ranch 
Recreation Special Revenue fund.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson voting “no”.  (#6146)   
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Downtown Development Authority (DDA) to generate 
revenue for general operating funds .  Amber Ash, Executive Director of DDA, explained that as 
part of the annual strategic planning process  and in effort to become financially stable, it was 
determined the DDA would seek approval of a resolution authorizing approval to generate revenue 
utilizing concepts such as advertising options, apparel sales, sponsorships as well as other revenue 
generating efforts.  She explained the DDA has faced challenges relating to unstable revenues over 
the last few years and they wish to look at different ways for sustainability.  Ms. Ash reported at 
the May 17th Finance Committee meeting, a recommendation to postpone the resolution to the next 
meeting of governing body was made.  In response to that, a substitute was drafted which removes 
flexibility for funding options leaving  specified options.  Wendy Volk  voiced support and 
applauded what the DDA has presented to council.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to June 14, 2021, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Dr. Rinne 
advised a substitute amendment was forthcoming and requested the postponement be voted down 
in order to consider the amendment.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “no” all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “yes”.   Dr. Aldrich moved to 
amend by substitute dated May 24, 2021, seconded by Mr. Roybal .  Mr. O’Donnell provided 
information on the substitute noting it removes the flexibility and only allows for funds to be raised 
for the listed items .  Comments and ad ditional details were provided on the proposed substitute 
amendment.  Concerns were raised relating to allowing full flexibility and the potential appearance 
of competition with merchants located downtown.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Collins, Mr. Johnson and Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”.  Additional concerns were raised regarding advertising , amount of available 
DDA reserve funding and turnover rate of executive directors and board members during the past 
6 years which contributes to the success of the DDA.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no”.  (#6147)

 Appointment of John B. Kopper as Chief of the Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-06-14 | CLIP_ID: 803 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 803

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 14, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel (via 
Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave  and Jeff 
White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be  no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda ).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 24, 2021 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and 
Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2022.  There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing.   
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist fo r land located northeast of the 
intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road.   Seth Lloyd, Planning & Development 
Department, provided a staff report and advised the area sought to be annexed contains 18 acres 
of land to be known as Meadowlark  Estates for mixed use development of commercial and 
residential dwellings.  Mr. Lloyd provided details relating to compliance of annexation criteria.  
There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor 
Collins closed the hearing.

Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal move d to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of

seconded by Mr. Segrave .  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governi ng body 
with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  (#4366) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic

 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Repealing Chapter 2.52, Personnel Commission, and 
creating a new Chapter 2.52, Personnel System, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Seth Lloyd , CPEA Board Member, advised that 
although there are concerns that still remain with the proposed changes to Chapt er 2.52 and 
Chapter 2.92, members of the CPEA support the proposed substitutes.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading , seconded by Mr. White.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by 
substitute dated June 2, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  V oting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Esquibel voting “no”. (#4368)

 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2021 and ending June 30, 2022.  Mr. White 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. White moved to 
amend by substitute dated June 10, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Charles Bloom, Planning and 
   
Development Director, explained that following discussion with the Compliance Director, it was 
determined the proposed code enforcement officer position should be moved to the Compliance 
Division rather than the Planning and Development Department .  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom 
reported the code enforcement officer would primarily perform enforcement duties relating to 
violations of the Unified Development Code (UDC) but may also assist in other code enforcement 
duties as necessary.  It was noted that upon approval, the position will be posted and filled when a 
suitable candidate has been identified.  Mr. White moved to amend the amendment by removing 
the position from the Planning and Development Department and placing it in the Compliance 
Division’s budget, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to amend the substitute carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  Motion to amend by substitute as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Discussion included concern wi th refilling 
positions that were a part of the fiscal year 2020 reduction in force, noting uncertainty with the 
city’s financial future.  Concern was voiced regarding management and enforcement of UDC and 
City Code requirements.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#4377)

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-06-28 | CLIP_ID: 805 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 805

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 28, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, C OUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook , Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave  and Jeff White.  
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motio n.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda ).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 14, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 ORDINANCE –EMERGENCY READING – Amending Section 10.80, Bicycles, of Title 
10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave). 
Mayor Collins advised that the City Attorney reported a large vendor of electronic scooters (e-
scooters) has plans to locate here beginning in July and has requested emergency approval of 3 
ordinances in order to provide for appropriate licensing and enforcement regulations prior to their 
arrival.  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, explained there are 3 proposed ordinances related to 
e-scooter and e-bicycle legislation.  He noted there are currently e-scooters operating in the city 
and it is anticipated there will be more companies coming to Cheyenne.  Mr. O’Donnell advised 
the ordinances focus on public safety particularly  noting the upcoming Cheyenne Frontier Days 
(CFD) event.  Upon request, Mr. O’Donnell reported on this agenda item and the 2 affiliated 
ordinances and advised this ordinance amends City Code Section 10.80 and i s a modification of 
the existing bicycle ordinance and includes definitions for e -scooters and related devices.  He 
advised Chapter 10.82 creates a regulatory structure for the devices that are for hire only, noting it 
specifically excludes the DDA Re-Ride Program.  He further reported it sets up the structure for a 
business license and fees, a series of requirements for how the business shall be run, insurance and 
indemnification requirements, suspension and revocation provisions that are established within the 
City Code administrative hearing process and public education required.  He noted e nforcement 
occurs through the Police Department, City Clerk, Compliance Division and occasionally through 
the Planning & Development Department.  It was further advised there are anticipated amendments 
to Chapter 10.82.  Mr. O’Donnell provided highlights of the proposed Indemnification Agreement 
which was reviewed by Bird Rides, Inc., a large operator planning to operate in Cheyenne, noting 
there are significant insurance and indemnification requirements.   Mr. O’Donnell explained 
amendments to Chapter 5.04 include adding the license in the business license section of the City 
   
Code.  Mayor Collins provided information relating to approval on Emergency Reading and 
explained a motion to suspend the rules is required, noting a three-quarters affirmative vote (8 out 
of 10 members of the Governing Body) is required to proceed for  emergency approval.  Mayor 
Collins advised if the motion to suspend the rules fails, the ite m would be referred to the Finance 
Committee for a recommendation.  Mr. White moved to suspend the rules and hear this ordinance 
as an emergency ordinance pursuant to Wyoming State Statute 15 -1-115(c), seconded by Mr.  
Roybal.  During public comment, Jeremiah Mahoney spoke in support of approving regulations 
prior to an influx of scooters being utilized by citizens.  Michael Covato, Bird Rides, Inc., Territory 
Manager, expressed enthusiasm for the opportunity to  locate in Cheye nne.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Covato advised the company has been internally assessing operations in Cheyenne over the last 
year and did outreach earlier this year, however formal conversations began several weeks ago.  It 
was noted from members of the Governing  Body that although there is excitement for the 
operation, th e emergency ordinance regulatory process  seems rushed and additional time to 
research and make appropriate decisions  is preferred .  Upon inquiry, Mr. Covato provided 
operations expectations and i nformation relating to CFD events , seasonal operations, and local 
management.  Mr. Covato expressed the desire to p artner with local members of the community 
to hire a local Fleet Manager.  Comments were made related to  time frame for scooters to arrive 
and time necessary to prepare required licensing and insurance provisions.  Upon further inquiry, 
Mr. Covato provided information on the number of e -scooters the company plans to start with  
locally which will be monitored for potential increases.  It was advised that geofencing regulations 
and limitations would be enforced, noting changing the area of the geofence is a simple process.  
Mr. O’Donnell noted the anticipated amendment defines geofencing that allows for flexibility at 
the discretion of the director, noting the director will work with companies to determine what those 
requirements should be.  Hamilton Byrd, local concert promoter, reported he and his partner have 
recently launched Bandwagon e-scooters in the community and they have been working with the 
Downtown Development Authority ( DDA) and the Metropolitan Planning Organization ( MPO) 
for several months.  Mr. Byrd reported the top speed of a scooter is limited to 20 kilometers per 
hour, helmets are suggested  and currently they have 32 e-scooters in operation.  He advised the 
MPO provided them with suggested geofencing requirements and noted the age minimum is 16 
for operation.  He further advised upon inquiry, that he supports regulations and will work with 
the city to become compliant  and confirmed alteration of geofencing is easily obtained.  Jenny 
Clough expressed s afety concerns  for riders and pedestrians  related to street and sidewalk 
conditions.  Ms. Clough voiced s upport of e-scooters but cautioned operations during C FD.   
Motion (to suspend the rules) carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  City Clerk Kris Jones read the ordinance entitlement into 
record upon request of Mayor Collins.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones explained that under 
the emergency ordinance procedure, a three-quarters affirmative vote (8 out of 10 members of the 
Governing Body) in order for the ordinance to pass.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by 
Mr. White.  It was noted by m embers of the governing body that the issues brought up by Ms. 
Clough demonstrates the need for requirements to be addressed immediately and that other cities 
have had to ban scooters as businesses came in without regulations in place.  Additional comments 
were made in support of efforts by the attorney’s office and support was voiced for the e-scooters 
but concern was expressed related to the  fast paced approval of the ordinances.  Mayor Collins 
noted occasionally the need arises for  approval of emergency procedures.    Upon inquiry, Mr. 
O’Donnell reported an ordinance exists that addresses skateboards in the Central Business District 
   
(CBD).  It was noted that in the future e lectric unicycle s will be considered for inclusion of 
regulations set forth within the affiliated ordinances.  Mayor Collins and Mr. O’Donnell confirmed 
that these vehicles would be allowed to operate on the greenway as  they are categorized by state 
statute as non-motorized due to engine size. Mot ion carried.  Voti ng “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#4378)  
 
 ORDINANCE –EMERGENCY R EADING – Creating a new Chapter 10.82, Motor -
Assisted Scooters, Electric Bicycles, and Bicycles Equipped with GPS, of Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave).  Mr.  White 
moved to suspend the rules and he ar this ordinance as an emergency ordinance pursuant to 
Wyoming State Statute 15-1-115(c), seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to appro ve, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. O’Donnell read into record the following 
proposed amendments:  Section 10.82.020-Definitions and Exclusions-addition of “Geofencing” 
means the use of a virtual perimeter in which the use of a dockless vehicle is either res tricted or 
the speed of the dockless vehicle is reduced when in use; 10.82.050-Dockless Vehicle Operations-
F-Notices of violations or broken or inoperable dockless vehicles: 1.  License holders shall have 
two hours after receiving notice of a violation , and 10.85.050(I):  License holders shall adhere to 
geofencing requirements, as determined and at the discretion of the director.  Mr. Segrave moved 
to amend by referenced language changes provided by Mr. O’Donnell, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided enforcement processes and confirmed the city would work 
within the spirit of the law for enforcement efforts.  M otion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” - all 
members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to July 12, 2021, seconded by Mr. 
Cook.  Support and  opposition was expressed for t he proposed postponement.  Jillian Harris, 
Transportation Planner for MPO, provided details on the history of efforts to implement legislation 
for regulations which began in February 2021.  Ms. Harris advised that introduction of e-scooters 
was inevitable and that the MPO has taken necessary steps to work with city staff and e-scooter 
operators to  protect the citizens.  Ms. Harris  explained the MPO had been working with the 
Bandwagon providers and Bird Rides, Inc. contacted the MPO in March and presented a 
memorandum of understanding ( MOU) which was catalyst to move th e legislation as quickly as 
possible in the best interest of the community from a public safety standpoint.  Michael Covato, 
Bird Rides, Inc., advised there are planning logistics that can’t be done until an ordinance outlining 
regulations is in effect, voicing opposition to  the postponement.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, upon 
inquiry, advised staff has been working on ordinance details including the business license 
component, noting final document details will require additional adjustments pushing 
implementation further out if postponed.  Mr. O’Donnell shared that coordination with insurance 
companies requires time to assure all requirements are in place, noting that a delay would most 
likely result in missing the timeline of being ready prior to CFD.  Mr. Johnson noted for the record 
he opposes rushed approval which denies the public opportunity to participate.  Mr. O’Donnell 
reported future ordinance amendments can take place after approval through the regular council 
approval process, noting the importance of having regulations in place prior to implementation of 
e-scooters in the community.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” - Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson 
and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no”  - Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. 
Roybal, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White.  M r. O’Donnell reported that the Indemnification and 
Insurance provisions will be finalized by staff, can be adjusted when necessary, and are not a part 
   
of the ordinance but are referenced in the ordinance.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by capping the 
number of e -scooters to a total of 80, seconded by seconded by Mr. Cook .  It was noted that 
Bandwagon already has 32 e-scooters active and that limiting the amount could raise legal concern 
with regard to restraint of trade.  Comments were made in support and  in opposition of the 
proposed amendment, and support was voiced for a free market approach.  Mr. O’Donnell voiced 
concern with a first come first serve environment  limiting those who can apply for a license to 
operate.  Tom Mason , MPO D irector, advised that w ithout rules and regulations in place , the 
program should be restricted to a pilot program  and that he anticipates use of e -scooters as mode 
of transportation, noting 80 scooters isn’t sufficient for that purpose.  Additional comments were 
made in support of letting the mar ketplace and company expertise determine the number  of e-
scooters the community needs.  Boyd Wiggam voiced opposition to capping the e-scooter amount 
and cautioned against restraint of trade noting he believes a cap would be detrimental in allowing 
this as a transportation type in the community .  He further voiced  support of e-scooters in the 
community.  Additional detailed comments were made in support and  in opposition of capping 
fleet numbers, free trade/market concept, restraint of trade, public safety concerns and uncharted 
territory relating to e -scooters.   Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “yes”.   Main motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson voting “no”.  (#4379)

ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 8.60.210, Liens, of Chapter 8.60, 
Nuisances, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Upon 
inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided clarification that the city would take priority of all liens except

comments were voiced, and it was noted this action is necessary to act responsibly in certain 
situations the city faces regarding public health and safety issues.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body.

line to recoup expenses.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

 Mr. Laybourn noted he will be sending each member of the Community Recreation & 
Events Department an email expressing appreciation for their efforts and hard work on the annual 
Superday event.  Mayor Collins, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave echoed Mr. Laybourn’ s comments 
and supports a staff email.   
 
 Dr. Aldrich advised she recently participated in a panel during affordable housing week 
and was impressed by a local contractor and the Planning & Development Department for their 
efforts.  She further noted supportive comments that were received by local area contractors 
related to Mr. Bloom’s leadership of the city’s Planning & Development Department.  She 
further provided information on a kick-off meeting for the new Technical Advisory Council and 
Innovations and Entrepreneurial Task Force that will be held on Tuesday, June 29th, beginning at 
5:30 p.m. at the Array Building.  
 
 Mr. Johnson commented on  a 13 -year-old he met during  Superday and expressed 
appreciation for his drive and work ethic noting he was raising awareness and funds for his church. 
  
  There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:10 p.m.  
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  July 9, 2021

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-07-12 | CLIP_ID: 807 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 807

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 12, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, C OUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich,  Ken Esquibel  (via Zoom) , 
Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:12 p.m.) , Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Tom Segrave  and Jeff White  (via Zoom).  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda ).  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Segrave and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #22 was removed from the Consent Agenda. 
Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 28, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive 
(Postponed from June 28, 2021).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, advised the 
annexation is dependent upon a county lot line adjustment which was denied and failed during an 
appeal.  The applicant is seeking reconsideration by the County Commissioners on July 20, 2021 
and staff and the applicant agree that postponing the item for 2 weeks would allow reconsideration 
instead of postponing indefinitely .  Mr. Lloyd noted this is affiliated with the next item on the 
agenda which is for the zone change.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone 
indefinitely, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted that city staff and the applicant have requested 
the motion to postpone indefinitely be voted down and instead postpone to the next meeting of the 
governing body.  Motion failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body present.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to postpone to July 26, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to postpone carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-Density Residential for land located southwest of the intersection of Braehi ll Road, 
Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive (Postponed from June 28, 2021).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve 
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone indefinitely, 
   
seconded by Mr. Segrave .  Mayor  Collins noted the desire of the staff and applicant is to vote 
down the motion to postpone indefinitely and instead postpone to the next meeting of the 
Governing Body on July 26, 2021.  Motion failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing 
body present.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to July 26, 2021, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion 
to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road. 
Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, explained the staff report indicated inclusion 
of the Dell Range right-of-way, however, there is c onsideration of including the right -of-way at 
the location due to WYDOT improvements. City staff including the City Engineer have confirmed 
the interest to include the right -of-way in the annexation and s taff has requested a 2-week 
postponement to allow for a map revision to include the right-of-way in the ordinance and affiliated 
map. Mr. Lloyd advised the postponement isn’t anticipated to delay the project tremendously as 
the final plat will still have to be submitted.  (It was noted for the record Bryan Cook, Councilman 
Ward II, joined the meeting at 6:12 during discussion and prior to vote on this item). Vicki Million-
Hughes provided a handout related to the annexation and project proposal for the land noting the 
developer would like to place 50+ homes on the land.  She commented on many concerns including 
obstructed views of the city skyline therefore requesting ranch style homes and buffers between 
properties, concern of what commercial property may be located on the land, accidents and traffic 
in the area and lack of green space in the subdivision.  It was noted the ordinance is to annex the 
land into the city and the related ordinance will create the zoning. It was advised that the final plat 
for development actions will be submitted at a later date.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to July 26, 2021, 
seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. Lloyd explained  upon inquiry that this project contained an 
agreement written into the original docume nts from county residential property owners on the 
hillside adjacent to the area  that the property down the hillside would be serviced by city sewer 
services and it would be anticipated to be developed for urban uses in the future.    Motion to 
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING –  Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use and County LR Low 
Density Residential to CB Community Business, LR Low-Density Residential, and MR Medium-
Density Residential for land loca ted northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range 
Boulevard, west of Whitney Road.  Patty Lockley requested a meeting with the  developer and 
nearby residents in the area be held prior to the next City Council meeting.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to 
July 26, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 8.60.210, Liens, of Chapter 8.60, 
Nuisances, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mayor 
Collins explained he had met with members of  the banking community and the Chamber of 
Commerce and the recommendation at this time is that the ordinance and the 2 following affiliated 
ordinances be postponed indefinitely.  Mayor Collins reported the effected parties will work on a 
   
solution for the lien situation  to be brought forward in the near future.  Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from the Committee.  It was noted 
a main motion is necessary prior to a motion to postpone.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave.  City Attorney Michael O’Donnell, upon inquiry, provided procedural information 
relating to the motion to postpone indefinitely explaining it essentially kills an item, however, 
allows for the item to be reintroduced at a later time. Dale Steenbergen, President/CEO for the 
Chamber of Commerce, confirmed his willingness to serve on a committee to find a compromise 
to the lien issue between the City and the financial community.  Conce rn was voiced relating to 
the motion to postpone indefinitely and it was noted that particular motion hasn’t been common 
practice for the governing body.  Motion to postpone  indefinitely carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 12.04.240, Liens, of Chapter 
12.04, Streets, Sidewalks, and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public 
Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Roybal advised there was no 
recommendation from the Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  
Motion to postpone indefinitely carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 15.24.030, Liens, of Chapter 
15.24, Dangerous Building Code, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from the Committee.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to postpone indefinitely carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various se ctions of Chapter 5.04, Business 
Licenses Generally, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4384) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.52, Massage 
Establishments, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4385) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 8.60, Nuisances, 
of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal advised 
there was no recommendation from the Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr . Segrave .  Mr. O’Donnell explained th e amendment brings the 
ordinance up to date with the new administrative hearing process. Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell 
explained that due to a miscommunication, this item did not receive a recommendation at the July 
   
6, 2021 Finance Committee meeting.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4386) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, Laramie County, 
Wyoming generally located southwest of Interstate 25 and Happy Jack Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted the annexation is unique due 
to the leased property being located adjacent to Warren Air Force Base (WAFB) and a request was 
made for additional information on the process.  Comments were made regarding lack of WAFB 
involvement in the annexation process, history of previous land uses at this location, current grant 
opportunities relating to utility development on th e site and information was provided regarding 
options for annexation of the land.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County X (Exempt) to SS (Support Services) 
for a portion of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, 
Laramie County, Wyoming generally located southwest of Interstate 25 and Happy Jack Road. Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Me dium-Density Residential to NR -2 
Neighborhood Residential -Medium Density for land located northwest of the intersection of 
Country Club Avenue and Snyder Avenue .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voti ng “yes” – all members of the governing body  
with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Public Safety Fee requirement for residential, commercial, service, industrial, civic, public 
service, employment, agriculture, and other development in the city.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members-Term-Vacancies, 
of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave. Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated July 6, 2021, seconded 
by Mr.  Segrave. Mr. O’Donnell explained the substitute provides for  initial Downtown 
Development Authority Board appointment to be made by the Mayor and adds a process to send 
suggestions to the Mayor for consideration.  Support was expressed for the process and it was 
noted this is needed to help achieve goals for the downtown district.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the g overning body with the exception of Mr. Cook and 
Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson voting “no”.   
   
 RESOLUTION – Approving propositions to impose the 1% Specific Purpose Sales and 
Use Tax in Laramie County, State of Wyoming, for the exclusive purpose of funding specific 
purposes for specific amounts; the propositions to be voted on at a special election to be held 
November 2, 2021.  Gunnar Malm, Laramie County Commissioner, explained that as of tonight, 
Laramie County and the towns of Albin, Pine Bluffs and Burns will have approved their resolutions 
and noted  he felt the p rocess went well , expressing appreciation to city staff.   Mayor Collins 
thanked Laramie County C ommissioners for their assistance during the process.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated July 
6, 2021 at 1:52 p.m. which includes the following amendme nts made at the July 6, 2021 Finance 
Committee meeting: clarification to proposition numbering and removal of the “17 th Street 
Lighting Project” in Proposition #10 and added the word “lighting” following the word 
“sidewalks” in Proposition #10, seconded by  Dr. Aldrich.  Opposition was voiced related to  the 
removal of the language “17th Street Lighting Project” in Proposition #10 and the determination to 
remove it without obtaining governing body approval .  Motion  to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  
Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the 
exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#6157) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Expressing the interest and support of the Governing Body in moving 
forward with the ‘Hyatt Place Hotel and Cheyenne Center’ Project .  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Comments were made regarding the nature of the proposal and  project 
including: city’s capacity to review the new proposal in various aspects of the development, vision 
for the project, public/private partnerships, future projects of this nature, opportunity to learn and 
look at what other municipalities are doing  related to similar projects, concern for future limited 
opportunities for development and appreciation was expressed to the proponents for their proposal 
and to members of the governing body for their willingness to consider the project.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6158) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to accept a grant award from the U.S. 
Department of Treasury for the American Rescue Plan Coronavirus State and Local Fiscal 
Recovery Funds in the amount of $12,191,813.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6159) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Community Recreat ion and Events 
Department to apply for a Small Business Administration Shuttered Venue Operators Grant to 
recoup a portion of revenue losses incurred as a result of mandatory coronavirus induced Civic 
Center closures.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6160) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events 
Department to apply for a Cheyenne Community Recreation District Recreation Mill Grant to 
purchase scoreboards and sound systems for the Cheyenne Junior League Athl etic Complex .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6161) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the expansion of the Capitol North Historic District and the 
addition of one hundred nineteen (119) structures to the contributing structures list maintained by 
the City’s Planning Office.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6162) 
 
   
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events 
Department to submit a proposal to apply for an Office of Local Defense Community Cooperation, 
Department of Defense, Defense Community Infrastructure Program Grant to build an indoor 
sports complex to support the military family quality of life Infrastructure of Francis E. Warren 
Air Force Base Service Members and their families – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – 
Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Waterford 
Square 3rd Filing, a replat of Lots 28 & 29, Block 7, Lots 1 & 31, Block 5 and a portion of Tray 
More Boulevard and a djacent alleys, Waterford Square, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located south of the intersection of Tray More Boulevard and Waterford Boulevard) – 
referred to Public Service Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Sweetgrass, 
7th Filing, a subdivision of a portion of the N½ of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6th P. M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie county, Wyoming (located south of East College Drive 
and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] Lease agreement between  XESI and the City of Cheyenne, I nformation Technology 
Department, to lease 1 copier for duplication services  (General Fund) .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#7367) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between Y2 Consultants, Jackson, WY, and the City of 
Cheyenne, Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO), for the East Pershing Boulevard Design 
Plan (MPO Federal Funds) .  Mo tion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$94,036.00). (#7368) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne , Cheyenne Police Department, and Laramie 
County School District No. 1 for law enforcement services at various schools and school-related 
events (Revenue to the City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7369) 
   
[CA] Memorandum of A greement between the 153D Airlift Wing, Wyoming Air National 
Guard, and the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department for Law Enforcement Assistance.  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7370) 
 
[CA] Easement and Assignment of Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Eco -
Site, LLC, Boca Raton, FL, for a tower, utility and access easement (Revenue to the City).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7371) 
 
[CA] Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming Department of 
Environmental Quality (WDEQ) for the installation and use of ambient air quality and 
meteorological monitors (Revenue to the City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7372) 
 
 
   
 Agreement between the City of Cheyenne  and the Cheyenne Animal Shelter/Services for 
Animal Control and Animal Shelter Services (General Fund).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an 
amount not to exceed $425,000, seconded by Mr . Segrave .  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by 
substitute dated June 30, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. O’Donnell explained the substitute 
noting it is for a period of time not to exceed 6 months  and payment is at the monthly rate the 
Cheyenne Animal Shelter requested from the city.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#7373) 
 
 Memorandum of Agreement between Robin and Debbie Courtier, the City of Cheyenne, 
and the Wyoming Department of Transportation for the study of downstream storm water 
problems.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. Roybal reported that  
due to a recent verbal agreement being reached and in an effort to expedite the use of the Detention 
Pond Easement, this item was reviewed under other items for the agenda at the July 6, 2021 
Finance Committee Meeting, therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City  Code have been met.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated July 7, 2021, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. 
O’Donnell reported the substitute includes  removal of  #10 under the “Now, therefore, in 
consideration of the mutual benefits and burdens as sumed by each party to this agreement” 
paragraph relating to costs that will be paid by the City, WYDOT and others.  He advised there 
may be an agreement for these fees in the future that will allow for the cost sharing to be determined 
at a later date.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Tom Cobb, City Engineer, was complemented 
for his efforts related to the memorandum of agreement and it was noted members of the governing 
body look forward to learning more on the process.  Comments were made that the draining issue 
has had substantial negative effect on how the city evaluates development projects relating to 
drainage concerns.  It was noted that drainage concerns have been expressed by property owners 
further downstream near Child’s Draw.  Main motion as amended carried.   Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr.  Cook and Dr. Rinne voting “ no”.  
(#7374) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County, Wyoming, and the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for EOD, SWAT, and CNU operations and extraterritorial law enforcement 
assistance and activities – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and SpyGlass Group, LLC, Westlake, OH, for 
communications services auditing – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Merchant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and RecTrac, LLC d/b/a Vermont 
Systems, for credit card processing of Recreation related services – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7231 between the City of Cheyenne and Burns and 
McDonnell for a force majeure for additional architectural and inspection services for the Landfill 
Front Entrance and Scale House Construction Support Project (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to 
Finance Committee.     
   
[CA] Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6625 between the City of Cheyenne and Western 
Research and Development, Ltd., for additional design services for the 2018 On Call Traffic 
Engineering Services Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Reappointment of Jay Sullivan to the Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint 
Powers Board.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote 
(mayoral appointments).   
 
 Announcement was made that the July 26, 2021 City Council meeting will be held in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, C heyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting at Noon instead of 6:00 p.m. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mayor Collins explained he has been working on the Urban Renewal Authority Board and 
has received all applications and would like to appoint the board members at this meeting to allow 
them to address the Hitching Post issue.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, read into the record the names for 
appointment:  Appointment of Katye Brown, Ken Dugas, Hugh Robert Graham, Chet Halvorson, 
and Ryan Whitehead to the Urban Renewal Authority Board.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral appointments).   
 
 Mayor Collins advised he was contacted on Friday , July 9, 2021 by the Fire Union to see 
if they could meet with him and the City Attorney relating to the Fire Department’s contract with 
the city.  Mayor Collins reported he would like to meet in Executive Session regarding the meeting 
held and noted there will be no further action taken after the meeting and that the regular meeting 
of the governing body will be adjourned following adjournment of the Execu tive Session.  Mr. 
O’Donnell advised there will be no further business after Executive Session and that adjournment 
of the regular meeting will not be on video.  Dr. Rinne moved to meet in executive session at 7:07 
p.m., seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion ca rried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
 Following adjournment of the Executive Session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular 
meeting of the governing body at 7:33 p.m. and there being no further business to come before the 
governing body, the meeting was officially adjourned at 7:34 p.m.  
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-07-26 | CLIP_ID: 809 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 809

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 26, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 12:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, C OUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich,  Ken Esquibel  (via Zoom) , 
Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson (via Zoom), Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne (joined via Zoom at 
12:06 p.m.), Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City 
Clerk.  
 
Following the pledge of allegiance, Mayor Collins called for a moment  of silence for 
Senator Mike Enzi related to injuries recently sustained in a serious bicycle accident.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted b y one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the ag enda. Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 12, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive 
(Postponed from July 12, 2021). Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Laybourn moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave . Mr. Laybourn advised that 
following the Laramie County Board of Commissioner’s meeting on July 20, 2021, the plat was 
reconsidered but not approved.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Mayor Collins explained the indefinite postponement essentially kills the item, therefore 
requiring it to come back before the governing body in the future.  Motion to postpone indefinitely 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-Density Residential for land located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, 
Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive  (Postponed from Ju ly 12, 2021).  Mr. Laybourn moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Laybourn advised that following 
the Laramie County Board of Commissioner’s meeting on July 20, 2021, the plat was reconsidered 
but not approved.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
to postpone indefinitely carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
   
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road . 
(Postponed from July 12, 2021).   Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Laybourn moved to amend by substitute dated July 16, 2021 , 
seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mayor Collins noted the substitute includes  the Dell Range Blvd.  
intersection on the map.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne vo ting “no”. [Clerks note: Dr. Rinne joined the 
meeting at 12:06 p.m. during discussion and prior to the vote on this item] (#4387) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use and County LR Low 
Density Residential to CB Community Business, LR Low-Density Residential, and MR Medium-
Density Residential for land located nort heast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range 
Boulevard, west of Whitney Road (Postponed from July 12, 2021) .  Mr. Laybourn moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4388) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, Laramie County, 
Wyoming generally located southwest of Interstate 25 and Happy Jack Roa d.  Mr. Laybourn 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4389) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County X (Exempt) to SS (Support Services) 
for a portion of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, 
Laramie County, Wyoming generally located southwest of Inters tate 25 and Happy Jack Road . 
Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr . White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4390)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium -Density Residential to NR -2 
Neighborhood Residential -Medium Density for land located northwest of the intersection of 
Country Club Avenue and Snyder Avenue .  Mr. Laybourn moved to approve  on third and final  
reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Charles Bloom, Planning and Development 
Director, reported the zone change request is affiliated with several different applications  and 
multiple variances were being considered by the Boar d of Adjustment including a reduction of 
parking, necessary setbacks and access of which several elements were denied.  Mr. Bloom advised 
work with the developer is ongoing to redo the site plan and plat .  He noted they are still 
recommending approval of the zone change to NR-2 as they believe it is the correct zoning for the 
neighborhood and will allow for existing development to continue and  for future redevelopment.  
Upon further inquiry, Mr. Bloom explained the developer will be retaining the existing building 
and improving the gravel parking lot  and noted they are proposing 4 new townhouse units along 
Snyder Ave.   Mr. Bloom advised t he Board of Adjustment denied th e request for 3 of the  
   
townhouse units to have rear access and 1 to have a driveway access on Snyder. During discussion 
related to comments and concerns of area neighbors, Mayor Collins noted he had received a 
records request for information related to the  project and comments were received related to 
drainage concerns and support for the added units but concerns with the space restraint .  Mayor 
Collins advised he met with the developer’s representative and they are aware of drainage issues 
and are working on an engineering solution.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom advised zoning in the area 
is MR (Medium Density Residential) and is complementary to the requested NR -2 zoning 
classification.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4391) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Public Safety Fee requirement for residential, commercial, service, industrial, civic, public 
service, employment, agriculture, and other development in the city .  Charles Bloom, Planning 
and Development Director, advised he met with members of the Southeast Wyoming Home 
Builders Association and although they aren’t  totally in support of the ordinance , they do agree 
this is an important first step and the ordinance w ill need to be revisited in the future .  Future 
review will involve the association and review of the fees to make sure they are accurate.  Mr. 
Bloom noted the fees will be for public safety infrastructure and not an offset to the Fire or Police 
Department’s general operating costs.   Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to the Southeast 
Wyoming Home Builders Association for their contributions related to the ordinance.  Mr. 
Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Comment was 
made related to the need to be able to maintain and provide public safety services in all areas of 
the community and appreciation was expressed to Mr. Bloom and the Southeast Wyoming Home 
Builders Association for working on the ordinance.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. (#4392)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members-Term-Vacancies, 
of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Comments were made regarding improvements that have been 
made downtown and the importance of the ordinance for streamlining downtown efforts.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception Mr. Johnson voting 
“no”.  Mayor Collins noted that due to the ordinance change, applications for the board will be 
accepted for appointment. (#4393) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events 
Department to submit a proposal to apply for an Office of Local Defense Community Cooperation, 
Department of Defense, Defense Community Infrastructure Program Grant to build an i ndoor 
sports complex to support the military family quality of life Infrastructure of Francis E. Warren 
Air Force Base Service Members and their families .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  
(#6163) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Waterford 
Square 3rd Filing, a replat of Lots 28 & 29, Block 7, Lots 1 & 31, Block 5 and a portion of Tray 
More Boulevard and adjacent alleys, Waterford Square, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located south of the inter section of Tray More Boulevard and Waterford Boulevard) . 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6164) 
   
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Sweetgrass, 
7th Filing, a subdivision of a portion of the N½ of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6th P. M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie county, Wyoming (located south of East College Drive 
and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive). Mr. Laybourn moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mr. 
Laybourn moved to postpone until August 9, 2021,  seconded by Mr. Segrave. Upon inquiry, 
Charles Bloom, Planning and Development Director, explained the reason for the postponement 
is to review a new plat that was submitted by the agent for the owner.  Motion to postpone carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing a one -time payment given to the full -time and permanent 
part-time employees of the City of Cheyenne (excluding elected officials and employees of the 
Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities) from funds received from the Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery Fund Established by the American Rescue Plan Act of 2021 – referred to Finance 
Committee.  (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Segrave, and Mr. 
White.)  
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County, Wyoming, and the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for EOD, SWAT, and CNU operations and extraterritorial law enforcement 
assistance and activities.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7376) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and SpyGlass Group, LLC, Westlake, OH, for 
communications services auditing.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7377) 
 
[CA] Merchant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and RecTrac, LLC d/b/a Vermont 
Systems, for credit card processing of Recreation related services.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#7378) 
 
[CA] Grant of Access Easement and Temporary Construction Easement on City owned Property, 
Tract U, Sweetgrass, 2 nd Filing, between the City of Cheyenn e and Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC  - 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Agreement between City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and Laramie County, 
Wyoming for juvenile probation services (revenue to the City) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7231 between the City of Cheyenne and Burns and 
McDonnell for a force majeure for additional architectural and inspection services for the Landfill 
Front Entrance and Scale House Construction Support Project (Solid Waste Fund).  Reporting for 
Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from committee.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $ 38,937.00, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Comments were made rel ating to the possibility of rebidding the project.  Upon inquiry, Craig 
LaVoy, Deputy Director of Public Works, explained the contract is for the project design and the 
consequence of requesting a new bid proposal is the project would be shut down.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. 
Esquibel voting “no”. (#7231) 
 
   
[CA] Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6625 between the City of Cheyenne and We stern 
Research and Development, Ltd., for additional design services for the 2018 On Call Traffic 
Engineering Services Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(in an amount not to exceed $35,194.51). (#6625) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-40-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Goat 
Green, LLC, for providing vegetation management for Dry Creek and Crow Creek, for the City of 
Cheyenne Engineering Department (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 Appointment of Julie Torres to the Laramie County Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board.   
Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral 
appointment).  
 
 Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held August 9, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers , 2101 O’Neil Avenue,  Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting, to receive comments and input for the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project, as 
required by Wyoming Statute §15-9-109, for land generally located northwest of West Lincolnway 
and Stinson Avenue.       
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mayor Collins noted that at the July 12, 2021 City Council meeting, the motion to approve 
the Memorandum of Agreement between Robin and Debbie Courtier, the City of Cheyenne, and 
the Wyoming Department of Transportation for the study of downstream water problems was 
approved. He further advised WYDOT was included in the MOU as they h old the easement for 
the pond  that is being reviewed , but that the Attorney General is uncomfortable signing the 
agreement.  Mayor Collins advised he would like a motion to reconsider the agreement to remove 
WYDOT and they will do a direct agreement with the city on the modification o f their easement.  
Mr. Segrave, having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant to City Code Chapter 2.04, moved 
to reconsider the Memorandum of Agreement between Robin and Debbie Courtier, the City of 
Cheyenne, and the Wyoming Department of Transportatio n for the study of downstream water 
problems, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Mr. Segrave moved to  amend 
by substitute dated July 23, 2021, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mayor Collins advised the only change 
in the substitute is the removal of WYDOT as a signatory of the agreement making the agreement 
between the city and the Courtiers only.  Upon inquiry, Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, advised 
the Attorney General ’s office has communicated concerns related to the agreement during a 
meeting and via email noting they aren’t comfortable signing the agreement in its original format.  
Upon request for more information on the project, Mayor Colli ns noted he will look into 
scheduling an informational work session.  Concern was raised  about WYDOT being removed 
   
from the agreement.  Mayor Collins described issues with the original agreement and noted he has 
received assurance that all parties want to be involved with the project.  Motion to amend by 
substitute caried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”.  Appreciation was expressed to the governing bod y for their consideration to 
remedy the situation.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#7374) 
 
 Dr. Aldrich expressed appreciation to Mr. Laybourn and the DDA for their beautification 
efforts in the downtown area.  She further thanked Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director of Community 
Recreation and Events, and his staff for their assistance with Fridays on the Plaza and city staff for 
their efforts downtown. 
 
 Mr. Laybourn noted the extra effort downtown was not unnoticed.  He further expressed 
appreciation to Public Works for the median clean-up by Little America.  Mayor Collins advised 
the median clean -up was a joint effort between Public Works and Community Recreation & 
Events.  Mayor Collins further expressed appreciation to city employees for their efforts in keeping 
the city safe and appealing. 
 
 Mr. Johnson noted the recent passing of Bill Dubois , local historian,  and expressed 
appreciation for all he did for the community. 
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 12:44 p.m.    
  
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kylie Soden, CMC  
Deputy City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  August 5, 2021
