MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-08-09 | CLIP_ID: 811 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 811

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 9, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 12:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, C OUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich,  Ken Esquibel, Bryan Cook, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne,  Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave  and Jeff White.  
Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda . Upon request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. White and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #9 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson, 
agenda item #16(a) wa s removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve 
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 26, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a p ublic hearing to receive comments and input for the Hitching 
Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project, as required by Wyoming St atute §15 -9-109, for land 
generally located northwest of West Lincolnway and Stinson Avenue.  Mark Christensen, Planning 
and Development  Department, provided information on the Urban Renewal Plan and Project 
stating the property has been declared as slum or blight by resolution which also created the Urban 
Renewal Area  (URA). Mr. Christensen provided information on the creation of a TIF (tax 
increment financing) district  which will allow for diverting a portion of taxes to help finance 
development in the area.  Mr. Christensen further provided information on what the proposed 
development will include and how much tax increments are anticipated to be received, noting staff 
recommends approval of the item.  Upon inquiry regarding why property south of Li ncolnway is 
included in the plan and project, Mr. Christensen explained when the blight study was originally 
conducted, it was unknown what the boundaries would be, but the Lincolnway right -of-way is 
included in case funding is available for improvements.  Melissa Burke, attorney from Hathaway 
and Kunz on behalf of Stephanie White and Dos ser, LLC, property owner in the corridor, read a 
letter on behalf of Ms. White into the record that provided history on the Hitching Post  property. 
Ms. White’s letter also noted she was unaware that her land to the northwest of the Hitching Post 
is included in the URA until last week  and acknowledged state statute doesn’t require landowner 
notification.  Ms. White  requested the URA to amend their bylaws.  There being no further 
comments or input, and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
   
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning c lassification from MR Medium -Density Residential to NR -2 
Neighborhood Residential-Medium Density for land located north of and adjacent to Miller Lane, 
between Sunset Drive and Townsend Place – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – 
Dr. Rinne) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Sweetgrass, 
7th Filing, a subdivision of a portion of the N½ of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6 th P. M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located so uth of East College 
Drive and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive)  (Postponed from July 26, 2021) .  Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend by substitute map dated July 21, 2021 , seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to amend by 
substitute map  carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   Main motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6165) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing a one-time payment given to the full -time and permanent 
part-time employees of the City of Cheyenne (excluding elected officials and employees of the 
Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities) from funds received from the Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery  Fund Established by the American Rescue Plan Act of 2021 .  Reporting for 
Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White. Comments were made 
and concerns were voiced related to hardships first responders and frontline workers faced during 
the pandemic including: working in Covid “hot zones”, childcare issues, increased workload for 
Sanitation workers, increased calls for domestic  related issues and employees riding in vehicles 
and equipment together with increased Covid exposure.  Motio n carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6166) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Approving the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and the Hitching Post 
Urban Renewal Project Pursuant to Wyoming Statute §15 -9-110, and approving the creation of a 
new Hitching Post Urban Renewal Authority Special Revenue fund – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Business 
Council under the Business Ready Community Grant and Loan Program for a Managed Data 
Center Cost Reduction Grant not to exceed $2,250,000.00 on behalf of the governing body of the 
City of Cheyenne – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities to discharge 
uncollectible debts in the amount of $1,138.02 pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §16 -4-502 – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2021 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W. S. §16 -4-112 & §16 -4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
   
[CA] Grant of Access Easement and Temporary Construction Easement on City owned Property, 
Tract U, Sweetgrass, 2 nd Filing, between the City of Cheyenne and Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7385) 
 
[CA] Agreement between City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and Laramie County, 
Wyoming for juvenile probation services (revenue to the City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (in an amount of $60,000.00 revenue to the City). (#7386) 
 
[CA] Contract between Laramie County and the Office of Youth Alternatives for on -call crisis 
center services in the amount of $69,000.00 (revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Event s, and the 
Officials for Fun Club to officiate the 2021-2022 youth volleyball, adult volleyball, and adult co-
rec volleyball programs (Recreation Programs Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Chey enne, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization and LSC Transportation Consults, Inc., Colorado Springs, CO, for the 2022 
Transit Development Plan (MPO Federal Funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #6 to Contract #5930 between the City of Cheyenne and Kone, Inc., 
Denver, CO, for the addition of an elevator located at the Judge Joseph M. Carey Cheyenne City 
Center Building (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
    
[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract #7256 between the City of Cheyenne and Dan Hart 
Patrol Service, LLC, Upton, WY, for the Happy Jack Landfill Phase 2, Cells 1, 2 and 3 Excavation 
Earthworks Project for an increase in rock excavation quantities and addition of Duke Energy 
Maintenance Road relocation (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7072 for the Hazard Mitigation grant from the 
Wyoming Office of Homeland Security, in the amount of $191,430.85 for Dry Creek Drainage 
Planning by extending the term of the grant agreement to August 5, 2022 (DHS FEMA Grant-Dry 
Creek Drainage Plan) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7309 between the City of Cheyenne and Synergy 
Construction, LLC, Lovell, WY, for the Happy Jack Landfill Front Entrance and Scale House 
Project to build an on -site scale house building (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance 
Committee.    
 
 Consideration of Bid #E-40-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Goat 
Green, LLC, for providing vegetation management for Dry Creek and Crow Creek, for the City of 
Cheyenne Engineering Department (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in 
an amount not to exceed $69,776.00, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Upon inquiry, Tom Cobb, City 
Engineer, advised the bid from Goat Green, LLC, was the only bid received and explained the cost 
increase as compared to previous contracts  may be related to revised base bid qualifications that 
include 5 years of experience and increased insurance requirements.  Mr. Cobb noted upon inquiry, 
   
that this type of vegetation management is an effective way to maintain landscape in flood areas.  
Upon further inquiry, Mr. Cobb and Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director of Community Recreation 
and Events, provided information on prior funding details for the service noting Weed and Pest 
used to assist in the funding but no longer has it in their budget.  It was noted that the Engineering 
Department and the Weed and Pest Division will work together on partnering for future funding.  
During comments related to vegetation in other drainage ways that need s to be addressed , City 
Attorney Michael O’Donnell advised there is an ordinance drafted for a stormwater drainage utility 
to be brought before the governing body in the near future.  Mr. Cobb, upon inquiry, advised he is 
working on updating the stormwater management plan including adding a task to the Dry Creek 
Master Plan  which includes reviewing the drainage ordinance.   Further comments were made 
regarding the importance of management of drainage ways and development of a stormwater 
drainage utility.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7387) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-1-22 for furnishing two (2) new 2021 4WD 72” front -mount 
mowers for City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events Department (2015-2018 1% Sales 
Tax and Specific Purpose Sales Tax Funding) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-23-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Kaiser 
Corporation d/b/a Kaiser Flooring, Cheyenne, for the Municipal Building 3 rd Floor Carpet 
Replacement Project (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.      
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held August 23, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting, to hear public comment regarding the intent of the City of Cheyenne to submit an 
application for funding for the Wyoming Business Council’s Business Ready Community Grant 
and Loan, Managed Data Center Cost Reduction Grant not to exceed $2,250,000.00 for reducing 
the communications and utility costs for three years through the reimbursement of accrued utility 
expenses and encouraging employment and private investment by Project Bison. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mayor Collins advised he would like to meet in Executive Session for the purpose of 
discussing a land transaction and noted there will be no further action taken after the meeting and 
that the regular meeting of the governing body will be adjourn ed following adjournment of the 
Executive Session.  Mr. White moved to meet in executive session at 6:35 p.m., seconded by Dr. 
Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 Following adjournment of the Executive Session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular 
meeting of the governing body at 6:55 p.m. and there being no further business to come before the 
governing body, the meeting was officially adjourned at 6:56 p.m.  
  
 
 
   
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  August 14, 2021

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-08-23 | CLIP_ID: 814 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 814

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 23, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel (via 
Zoom) Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne,  Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave  and Jeff 
White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda. City Clerk Kris Jones noted agenda item #14 was inadvertently referred 
to the Public Services Committee and is instead referred to the Finance Committee.   (All agenda 
items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be routine items by the governing body 
and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate discussion on these items unless a 
member of the governing body so requests and support by two other members is received.  Any 
item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal sequence on the agenda. 
Upon request by Mr. Roybal, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Segrave and Dr. Aldrich, 
agenda item # 10 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Roybal , and 
pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave, agenda item #17(b) was removed 
from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items , seconded by  Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 9, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input regarding the intent 
of the City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding for the Wyoming Business Council’s 
Business Ready Community Grant and Loan, Managed Data Center Cost Reduction Grant not to 
exceed $2,250,000.00 for reducing the communicatio ns and utility costs for three years through 
the reimbursement of accrued utility expenses and encouraging employment and private 
investment by Project Bison.  There being no comments or input, and purpose of the hearing having 
been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium -Density Residential to NR -2 
Neighborhood Residential-Medium Density for land located north of and adjacent to Miller Lane, 
between Sunset Drive and Townsend Place.  Tammy Plunkett, adjacent property owner, expressed 
concerns with the proposed zone change involving possible drainage and sewer issues, increased 
traffic, proposed type of development and the planned number of additional townhome residential 
units.  Ms. Plunket t expressed concerns with the proposed removal of Dean Court, which is the 
only access road that goes along Miller Lane , relating to emergency medical services (EMS) and 
police access to the area.  She noted she believes there is an error in the city’s staff report relating 
to blocks mentioned in the development and requested consideration to keep the current  zoning.  
   
Brad Emmons, AVI, and developer for the project, provided information about the conceptual plan 
for the area which includes a twin home development with a shared open space access area.  Mr. 
Emmons explained the purpose of requesting the NR-2 zoning is to allow for shared open space, 
not for an increase of residential units, noting units will have a deck and will share the common 
open space.  Mr. Emmons advised this is a new development concept, an HOA will maintain 
landscaping and the plat will go before the governing body  for approval.  Upon i nquiry, Mr. 
Emmons reported a traffic study is not required as the area is  below the threshold  requirement.  
Mayor Collins noted the development is currently in the design phase for drainage and will likely 
include 2 detention ponds.  Concerns were raised related to increase of area traffic  and street, 
gutter, and sidewalk improvements/remediation.  Mr. Emmons reported he has met with the City’s 
Engineering Department on th e concerns and provided information on existing area  curb, gutter 
and sidewalks and noted some alternatives are being reviewed in terms of curb and gutter.  Upon 
inquiry, he reported the proposed access easement is 28’ which has been vetted with the city’s Fire 
Department for being acceptable for access.  Following additional inquiry, Mr. Emmons provided 
information on options that were discussed relating to sanitation services and placement and types 
of waste receptacles relating to development density and building type.  Mr. Emmons advised open 
space will be for HOA property owners and their guests and is not intended for public use.   Leroy 
Baluska, adjacent property owner, requested consideration to keep the current zoning and noted 
concerns including infill development, water runoff and drainage issues, increased traffic and 
design concept.    Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  It was noted by members of the governing body that while 
understanding the concerns raised, the property could be developed with 18 multi-residential units 
without the proposed zone change to NR-2 which is for the purpose of allowing for open space.  It 
was further noted that the developer will have to come back with the plat and prove concerns have 
been taken into consideration.  Explanation was provided regarding the purpose of infill 
development which is intended to fill open unused space.  Mr. Emmons clarified that although 
there may be a need to bring in extra dirt for the development, the intent is to keep the area as close 
to what is existing as possible.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.   
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
clarify and better facilitate cottage lot development – referred to Public Services Committee.

moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. Laybourn advised that although he does not 
believe he has a conflict restricting him from voting on the appointments, for the purpose of public 
perception, he will be abstaining from voting.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins  who did no t vote (mayoral appointments) 
and Mr. Laybourn who abstained. 
 
 
   
 Under other business: 
 
 Announcement was made that due to City offices being closed for the Labor Day holiday 
on Monday, September 6, 2021, regularly scheduled city Council Committee meetings will instead 
be held in the Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and 
viz electronic conference meetings as follows:  Finance Committee will meet Tuesday, September 
7, 2021 at Noon and Public Services Committee will meet Wednesday, September 8, 2021 at Noon.   
 
 Memorandum of Agreement between Robin and Debbie Courtier, the City of Cheyenne, 
and the Wyoming Department of Transportation for the study of downstream storm water 
problems.  Mayor Collins advised the city received the memorandum of agreement ( MOA) this 
afternoon with signatures and noted this agreement is somewhat different than the prior agreement.  
Mayor Collins referred the item to Public Services Committee.   
 
 Appointment of Stefanie Boster as City Attorney for the City of Cheyenne.  Mayor Collins 
announced Michael O’Donnell, current City Attorney, will retire on September 3rd and that he is 
pleased Ms. Boster has agreed to accept the position of City Attorney.  Mayor Collins advised Ms. 
Boster comes to the City with unique ideas as she was a previous council member for the City of 
Casper and has several years of valuable experience practicing law.  Mr. White moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote  (mayoral appointment).  City Clerk Kris Jones 
administered the oath of office to Ms. Boster. 
 
 Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to Mr. O’Donnell for his years of service to the City 
and advised this is his last council meeting as City Attorney.  Mayor Collins wished Mr. O’Donnell 
and his wife the best in their upcoming retirements.       
 
 Mr. Laybourn reported on a recent clean-up at the underpass of Lincolnway and Missile 
Drive, adjacent to MLK Park , and expressed appreciation to Councilman Johnson, city staff 
members Eric Fountain, Compliance Director, David Singleton, Assistant City Attorney, and 
members of his family, John Palmer, Compliance  Department, and members of the public 
Cameron Karajanis, Brock Lawrence and Holly Frank.  He explained the need for members of the 
governing body and the public to be aware of the magnitude of the problem along Crow Creek and 
that he feels the issue needs to be taken more seriously.  He advised this type of inha bitation has 
serious environmental impacts to the nearby Crow Creek.  He further noted that when homeless 
encampments occur it is hard to undo them as it is emotional for everyone involved  especially 
when children are affected.  Mr. Laybourn requested consideration for the city to begin discussions 
with the Wyoming Department of Transportation for the purpose of developing a partnership in 
these types of areas to find some sort of solution and noted that if not, the issues will continue to 
occur.  Further discussion included the on-going efforts to redevelop blighted areas and the need 
to provide reasonable housing options to the homeless.  Mr. Johnson expressed appreciation to the 
Police Department for their assistance during the clean-up.     
  
 There being no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:00 p.m.   
   
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-09-13 | CLIP_ID: 816 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 816

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 13, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn  (arriving at 6:05 p.m.) , Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom 
Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was 
recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one mo tion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  M r. White moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne 
voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 13, 2021.   Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium -Density Residential to NR -2 
Neighborhood Residential-Medium Density for land located north of and adjacent to Miller Lane, 
between Sunset Drive and Townsend Place.  Tammy Plunkett, adjacent property owner, reiterated 
comments and concerns voiced at the previous city council meeting related to the proposed zone 
change involving possible drainage and sewer issues, increased traffic, proposed type of 
development and the planned number of additional townhome residential units.  Ms. Plunkett 
expressed concerns with the proposed removal of Dean Court, which is the only access road that 
goes along Miller Lane, relating to emergency medical services (EMS) and police access to the 
area.  She noted she believes there is an error in the city’s staff report relating to blocks mentioned 
in the development and requested consideration to keep the current zoning.  Ms. Plunkett advised 
she has attended several meetings related to the proposed zone change and has talked to neighbors 
in the area.  Upon request by Mayor Collins for examples of mistakes made related to the proposed 
zone change as referenced by Ms. Plunkett, she noted the discrepancy in lots in the original staff 
report (which has been corrected) and that she doesn’t believe her concerns have been addressed  
as the proposed project will affect her and her property.  Ms. Plunkett requested consideration of 
tabling the matter.  Brad Emmons, AVI, and agent for the applicant, provided information on the 
cottage lot development type noting it has been in the community for some time.  Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Emmons explained the lot numeration error has been corrected in the staff report and many of 
the voiced concerns such as traffic, sewer, drainage, and emergency services access  have been 
discussed.  Mr. Emmons further advised the project’s sewer and water are being reviewed by the 
   
Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) and the sewer line up Dean Court will be abandoned as it doesn’t 
function and was previously a private line, therefore a new sewer line and water main will be 
installed.  Mr. Emmons noted the proposed project will include 18 1 bedroom 1 bath units and a 
traffic study has been turned in  as required by the city.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Emmons 
explained the proposed amendment to the cottage lot development ordinance will affect the 
development and provided information on the ordinance amendment noting there are many 
requirements for this development type.  If the amendments to the cottage lot development 
ordinance aren’t approved, they would likely need a new concept under NR-2 zoning classification 
for the proposed project.  It was explained that during the final plat stage of the project, engineering 
drawings and additional exterior elevation requirements are submitted.  Tom Cobb, City Engineer, 
advised they have received a preliminary report for drainage for the infill development and final 
determination on drainage will be made during the next development step .  Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  
Johnson. Comment was made that under the current zone class ification of MR, the proposed 18 
units can be built but changing the zoning classification to NR -2 will allow the cottage lot 
development type which will include shared common outdoor space where Dean Court is currently 
located.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of 
Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#4394)   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
clarify and better facilitate cottage lot development.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve o n second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Comments were made related to the current requirements of 
the cottage lot development being hard to meet and constituents concerns with patio type homes 
that use this type of lot.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Defining the Terms and Conditions of Employment for 
Sworn Officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 2021 -2022 – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 2.76.020(B), Members -Terms-
Vacancies, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services 
Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AG Agricultural to MR Medium -Density 
Residential, HR High -Density Residential, MUB Mixed -Use Business Emphasis, and CB 
Community Business for land located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range 
Boulevard and Whitney Road – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne)     
 
 RESOLUTION – Permitting the possession of open containers within the Downtown 
District Boundaries for the ‘Golf, it’s the Least of our Problems’ Golf Tournament to be held on 
September 25, 2021.  Jeff White recused himself from discussion and voting due to  a family 
member’s employment affiliation with the Cheyenne Depot Museum and vacated council  
chambers.  Bryan Grzegorczyk provided information about the event noting it is a putt putt style 
   
golf tournament throughout several downtown businesses  that is a fundraiser for the Cheyenne 
Depot Museum and also brings business downtown .  Mr. Grzegorczyk stated 320 people 
participated in the event last year and over $18,000 was raised.  Upon inquiry, Kaylan Eicholtz , 
Cheyenne Depot Museum, noted there weren’t any open container issues or concerns with the 
event last year (or generally relating to open containers in the downtown area) and the request for 
permitting open containers is mainly so the tournament participants can carry their drinks 
throughout the tournament.  Cheyenne Police Department Chief Mark Francisco explained upon 
inquiry that there were no major problems with the open container allowance this year and the 
department can utilize the option of public intoxication citations when necessary. Additional 
comments were made related to the function of last year’s tournament and how some teams didn’t 
get to finish the game.  Mr. Grzegorczyk noted the event will be scaled down and only 2 teams 
will be at each hole.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted the request 
for permitting open containers for the event is due to the event being scheduled outside of the 
Memorial Day to Labor Day open container timeframe that is allowed in the downtown district 
boundaries.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6171)

 Notice of Emergency Procurement.  In accordance with the City of Cheyenne Purchasing

 Under other business: 
 
  Mr. Johnson provided information on the Rooted in Cheyenne organization and advised 
they are looking for an additional 40 volunteers for an upcoming tree planting event to be held on 
September 18, 2021. 
 
 Mr. Johnson noted that due to the city’s recent naming policy changes, he began research 
to determine if there are any Cheyenne parks named after women , of which there are none. He 
requested this be considered if there are any acquired parks that need named.  
 
 Dr. Aldrich recognized the attendance of Lynn Huylar, Director of Safe Harbor and Erin 
LeBlanc, Director of Senior Services, and expressed appreciation for their services. 
 
 There being  no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:40 p.m.   
   
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  September 22, 2021

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-09-27 | CLIP_ID: 818 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 818

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 27, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken  Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  
Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by Mr . Johnson, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #14 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave, 
agenda item # 21(c) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve 
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 13, 2021.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried.  
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
clarify and better facilitate cottage lot development. Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development  
Department, explained the proposed ordinance and amendment stating individual units also known 
as cottage and bungalow courts, contain a common outdoor open space.  He provided existing 
community examples of the development type noting they are townhome style units. Mr. Lloyd 
explained the objective of the ordinance proposal is to update current cottage lot type development 
regulations to allow for utilization of this type of development.  One current challenge is that there 
are only  10 units  allowed per development, which the amendment proposes to remove that 
restriction. Mr. Lloyd advised a proposed substitute ordinance will address a few issues that 
weren’t originally included in the ordinance amendment and will clean up language and formatting 
making it easier to read.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lloyd explained the Planning and Development 
Department asked developers to review the existing cottage lot development regulations and 
provide feedback on issues and concerns. Regarding landscaping requirements, Mr. Lloyd stated 
cottage lot developments will require site plans that will address landscaping and will give more 
weight to water-wise landscaping.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Lloyd speculated existing cottage lot 
developments may have been reviewed and processed as apartment complexes initially.  Mr. Lloyd 
further noted the exist ing cottage lot developments are townhome style  developments and the 
proposed amendment will allow single family, townhome  and multi-family developments.   
Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Division Chief Byron Mathews, upon inquiry, provided information on 
emergency services access noting with the development type, a 26’ access will be required on both 
sides of developments.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated 
September 24, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4395) 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Defining the Terms and Conditions of Employment for 
Sworn Officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 2021 -2022.  Reporting for 
Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. Aldric h.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020(B), Members -Terms-
Vacancies, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, second ed by Mr. Segrave. Upon inquiry, Deputy City Attorney  Alessandra McCoy 
Fakelman confirmed state statute indicates the mayor makes Downtown Development Authority 
(DDA) board appointments that includes a member of city council  which requires the governing 
body’s confirmation. She advised this  board differs from others as the council member  
representative is a voting member  and the change being proposed is for the council president to 
make the appointment with confirmation of the governing body.  Comment was made related to a 
requested upcoming review of the 22 boards and commissions that have council representation 
and how they function.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. McCoy Fakelman  provided the following 
information: not all board and commission memb ers are appointed by the mayor , but the statute 
related to this board specifically says members are  appointed by the mayor  and the council 
president doesn’t currently have authority to remove that appointed member.  Discussion followed 
and included the following topics: support for appointments on the DDA board remaining with the 
mayor, mayor’s authority to remove members from boards, history on a prior scenario involving 
a matter resulting in the suggested resignation of a member, the process of appointing  a council 
representative to boards that have a council representative , purpose of the ordinance amendment 
is to align with the historical practice of council representative appointment and that appointments 
will still be confirmed by the governing body.   Ms. McCoy Fakelman advised that because the 
DDA board is drafted differently than most boards, the statute says a member can be removed by 
governing body, confirming that would still be the case if the amendment is approved.  Ms. McCoy 
Fakelman explained upon inquiry that the ordinance amendment was reviewed by the previous 
city attorney and the recommendation was that the amendment could be made based on statute 
language. Discussion continued on the purview of the mayor appointing board members,  home 
rule, attorney’s office review of the ordinance prior to third and final reading and reports required 
to be presented to the governing body by boards.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AG Agricultural to MR Medium -Density 
Residential, HR High -Density Residential, MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis, and CB 
Community Business for land located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range 
Boulevard and Whitney Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
clarify the interpretation of Lots and amend Accessory Building Regulations – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
modifying the Density Standards of U DC 5.1.5D “Multi-Dwelling Buildings (Apartment)” Lot 
Types – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne)    
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
modify the MUR, MUB, and MUE zone districts’ intent, context, and transitions – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2022 Budget o f the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16 -4-112 & 16 -4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6174) 
 
 RESOLUTION – To honor William R. ‘Bill’ Dubois and his contributions to the arts and 
humanities in Cheyenne, Wyoming. Dr. Rin ne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mr. 
White, Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Johnson and Mr. Laybourn expressed appreciation to Mr. Dubois and his 
historian and philanthropy contributions in the community over the years.  It was requested that a 
framed copy of the resolution be presented to Mr. Dubois’ family.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
- all members of the governing body. (#6175) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Miller 
Addition, 5th Filing, a replat of Lots 7 -18, Block 3 and Dean Court, Miller Addition, Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of Miller Lane, between Sunset Drive and Townsend 
Place).  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt with staff condition #1 to note ownership of Lots 1, 14 and 21, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, 
explained the staff condition is for  the ownership of common lots. Mr. Lloyd reported that the 
adjacent land-owner’s concerns previously expressed will be addressed during the site plan phase 
of the project  and that all  project elements will be required to meet city code.   Concern was 
expressed related to adjacent land -owner’s concerns being addressed.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the go verning body  with the exception of Mr. L aybourn voting “no”. 
(#6176) 
 
[CA]  RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for the owner of the 
property located at 5057 Loving Trail to allow installation of an on -site septic system located 
within the city.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6177) 
 
[CA]  RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a final plat for Whitney 
Ranch, 4th Filing, a plat of a portion of the southeast quarter of Section 23, Township 14 North, 
Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of and 
adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road) – referred to Public

[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting liquor license general guidelines for the review and evaluation 
of applications for a retail liquor license available through the City of Cheyenne, and establishing 
a time frame in which applications may be submitted to the City Clerk – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Permitting the possession of open containers within the Downtown 
District Boundaries for the DDA “Spirits & Spirits” Halloween Pub Crawl to be held on October

[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board in the submission of

Rescue Plan Act of 2021 to assist in fun ding costs related to operations, cleaning, sanitization, 
janitorial services, combating the spread of pathogens at the airport, and debt service payments – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for Occupant Protection High Visibility Overtime -Traffic Enforcement 
(Highway Safety and Occupant Protection Hi -Vis Enforcement Grants).  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#7413) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for Impaired Driving High Visibility Overtime -Traffic Enforcement (Highway 
Safety and Impaired Driving Hi-Vis Enforcement Grants).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#7414) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Tourism Promotion 
Joint Powers Board, d/b/a Visit Cheyenne, regarding the partnership to develop planning and 
design for a first phase of the Cheyenne Railroad Visit or Experience.   Deputy City Attorney  
Alessandra McCoy Fakelman advised the Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is with the city 
and Visit Cheyenne to manage the first phase of the Cheyenne Railroad Visitor Experience Project.  
Ms. McCoy Fakelman provided project information and noted the City Engineer’s office is 
working in conjunction with Visit Cheyenne on the project.  Comment was made that before grant 
opportunities can be reviewed for the project, the first phase of the project must be completed. 
Domenic Bravo, Visit Cheyenne CEO, provided further information on the project noting it is part 
of the master plan that was unveiled in June of 2021 and the idea is to have a project manager to 
ensure the project is moving forward.   Brian Casey, Mr. B’s Heating & Cooling, stated they 
recently purchased a building at 15th and Bent and expressed support for the project and downtown 
development.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $ 120,000.00, seconded 
by Mr. White.  Comments were made related to the project funding source and appreciation was 
expressed to those who have worked on the project’s vision.  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Robin 
Lockman advised the funding source for the project is the annexation loan fund  which contains 
about $250,000.00 and noted t here is a resolution that was adopted in  2010 that regulates and 
explains how the money in this fund can be spent.  Upon inquiry, City Engineer Tom Cobb advised 
currently the project’s first phase estimated total amount is $88,900.00.  During discussion related 
to funding language in paragraph #3(m), Dr. Rinne moved to delete paragraph #3(m), seconded 
by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – members of the governing body.  Upon 
inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones noted the intent of the motion is clear to remove paragraph #3(m) 
which will result in renumbering remaining items.  Appreciation was expressed to Mr. Bravo for 
reaching out to businesses on 15th Street to get their buy in related to the project. Comments were 
made related to downtown improvements, private investments and it was noted the first project 
walkabout review will be Friday, October 1 st, beginning at 5:00 p.m. During comments made 
related to  the DDA board council representative, Mayor Collins raised a point of order that 
comments were not germane to the MOU.  Further comments were made related to an upcoming 
work session to the be held on October 8 th and community inquiries related to relocating the Big 
Boy steam engine from Holiday Park.   Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#7415) 
 
[CA] Rental Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events 
Department, and the Cheyenne Capitals Youth Hockey Association for use of the Cheyenne Ice 
and Events Center (Revenue to the city).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7416) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events Department, 
and the Cheyenne Capitals Youth Hockey Association to authorize the purchase of advertising 
space inside the arena of the Cheyenne Ice and Events Center as identified (Revenue to the city).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7417) 
  
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and BM C One, Inc., to provide SIP trunk

[CA] Purchase and sale agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Jerilou Lockhart to acquire

[CA] Agreement between the Wyoming D epartment of Health, Aging Division, to provide 
support services to persons 60 years or older under the Title III -B Program Grant for Fiscal Year 
2021 (Revenue to the city) (III-B Division on Aging Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Russel + Mills 
Studios, Inc., for the East Cheyenne Community Park Master Site Development Plan (Specific

[CA] Agreement between the City o f Cheyenne, Laramie County Sheriff’s Office and Laramie 
County, Wyoming, for housing prisoners at the Laramie County Detention Center (General Fund) 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -2-22 for a contract between the City of Chey enne and Reiman 
Corp, for the LCCC Greenway Underpass Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax, WYDOT TAP 
Grant & WYDOT STPU Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -3-22 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corp for the Carey Avenue Multi Use Path (Specific Purpose Sales Tax & WYDOT TAP Grant) 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 Appointment of Davi d E. Singleton to Municipal Court Part -Time Hearing Judge. Mr.  
Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Concern was expressed related to a recent 
public censure of Mr. Singleton from the Wyoming State Bar. City Attorney Stefanie Boster noted 
the censure is the purview of the State Bar and has been addressed and handled.  Ms. Boster and 
Mayor Collins both expressed support for the appointment of Mr. Singleton.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. 
Johnson voting “no” and Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral appointment).  City Clerk Kris 
Jones administered the oath of office to Mr. Singleton. 
 
 Reappointment of Kristin Lee to the Cheyenne Housing Authority.  Mr. W hite moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral appointment).   
 
 Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held October 1 1, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Bijou, Inc. 
d/b/a Capitol Cinema 16, 3410 Concord Rd., Cheyenne, WY 82001.     
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Johnson advised he has been contacted by downtown property owners in the 
Downtown Development Authority District (DDA) who felt they weren’t properly informed when 
the DDA and Visit Cheyenne merged.  He advised the property owners would like transparency 
and to be provided information moving forward.  
  
 Dr. Aldrich provided information related to a concern raised to her from a resident related 
to lack of public information for a process change involving discarding wild game carcasses.  She 
advised there previously was a dumpster for this use located outside of the transfer station which 
has been removed. 
 
Mayor Collins requested the governing body meet in executive session to discuss a F ire 
Union litigation update and  a potential property acquisition. Mr. Segrave moved to meet in 
executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-405(a)(iii) and 16-4-405(a)(vii), to consider 
or receive any information classified as confidential by law, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mayor Collins announced members 
would meet in executive session, not open to the public; requested the Deputy City Attorney, City 
Clerk, City Treasurer and whomever the City Attorney’s office has approved to be present and  
stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive session, 
and that the regular City Cou ncil meeting would officially conclude at the close of the executive 
session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 7:26 p.m.).     
 
 Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 8:31p.m.   
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:32 p.m.   
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-10-11 | CLIP_ID: 820 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 820

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 11, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Richard Johnson, Pete 
Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne  (via Zoom) , Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.   Absent:  
Bryan Cook and Ke n Esquibel.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 Mayor Collins called for a moment of silence for Cliff Abraham, a Transit Department 
employee, who recently passed away. 
 
 PROCLAMATION-Designating October of 2021 as Community Planning Month.  Mayor 
Patrick Collins read the proclamation into the record in its entirety and provided details involving 
awards that were presented to staff members of the Planning  and Development Department for 
group and  individual honors.  Charles Bloom, Planning  and Development Director, provided 
congratulatory comments to his staff for their accomplishments.   Members of the Governing Body 
expressed appreciation to staff for their efforts. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by Mr . Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. White and Dr. Aldrich, agenda items #25(b) and #25(d) were removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson 
and Mr. White, agenda item #27(a) was removed from the consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 27, 2021.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Bijou, Inc. d/b/a Capitol Cinema 16, 3410 Concord Rd., Cheyenne, 
WY 82001.  Robert Berger, Capitol Cinema  16, provide d details relating to the proposed 
dispensing and consumption areas which include the Club 21 area, movie auditoriums and dining 
area.  Mr. Berger advised the business plan is to serve single serve malt beverage and wine products 
only from one of their two proposed dispen sing areas and noted staff will be TIPS trained.   
Appreciation was expressed for Capitol Cinema 16 remaining in business during the pandemic and 
concern was expressed related to alcohol being allowed in all movie auditoriums.  There being no 
further comments or input, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Defining the Terms and Conditions of Employment for

Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4396)

 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and BMC One, Inc., to provide SIP trunk 
communication services to the city for cost savings on analog phone circuits used for 911 calls at 
outlying city offices (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to 
exceed $10,764.00). (#7424) 
 
 Purchase and sale agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Jerilou Lockhart to acquire 
real property (250,470 square feet along Railroad Right of Way East of Raleigh Drive) for 
expansion of the Greenway (Specific Purpose Sale Tax) . Mr. Roybal moved to approve  in an 
amount not to exceed $240,000.00, seconded by Mr. White. Upon inquiry,  Jeanie Vetter, 
Greenway and Parks Planner, provided information rela ting to th e property acquisition, project 
funding source, and explained there will be additional acquisitions relating to the project.  Charles 
Bloom, Planning and Development Director, provided additional information involving property 
zoning.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#7425) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the Wyoming Department of Health, Aging Division, to provide 
support services to persons 60 years or older under the Title III -B Program Grant for Fiscal Year 
2021 (Revenue to the city) (III-B Division on Aging Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#7426) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Russel + Mills 
Studios, Inc., for the East Cheyenne Community Park Master Site Development Plan (Specific 
Purpose Sale Tax) .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve  in an amount not to exceed $83,260.00, 
seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Jeanie Vetter, Greenway and Parks Planner, upon inquiry, provided 
information on the request for proposal process as regulated by the City’s Purchasing Policy.  Ms. 
Vetter advised Russell + Mills scored the highest of  three (3) proposals received.  Discussion 
included estimated project start time of October 2021 and drainage design for underpasses and 
tunnels. Upon inquiry, Tom Cobb, City Engineer,  provided funding information which includes 
submitting grant applications and advised the preliminary design has been submitted to the Union 
Pacific Railroad for review.  Discussion included:  Desire for a public swimming facility on the 
east side of town, review and remediation of any area drainage issues, and support for completion 
of one of City Council’s annual goals.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  (#7427) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County Sheriff’s Office and Laramie 
County, Wyoming, for housing prisoners at the Laramie County Detention Center (General Fund).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7428) 
 
[CA] Human Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and United Way of Laramie 
County to provide services to various community programs (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of Transportation 
(WYDOT) for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Grant to assist in providing a local match 
for operating funds for the Transit Program for Fiscal Year 2022 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 2021-2022 Collective Labor A greement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and 
Cheyenne Firefighters, Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7287 between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne 
Metropolitan Planning Organization ( MPO), and Benchmark Engineers, PC, Cheyenne, for 
additional professional services for the Walterscheid Boulevard Reconstruction Plan (MPO 
Federal Funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Consideration of Bid #S -2-22 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corp for the LCCC Greenway Underpass Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax, WYDOT TAP 
Grant & WYDOT STPU Grant) .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$1,923,333.40, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Tom Cobb, Cit y Engineer, and Jean ie 
Vetter, Greenway and Parks Planner, provided project information involving funding partnerships 
and grant opportunities and current and future Greenway connection proposals in the area.  Motion 
carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#7429) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -3-22 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corp for the Carey Avenue Multi Use Path (Specific Purpose Sales Tax & WYDOT TAP Grant).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $806,008.55). (#7430) 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Appointment o f Roberta Coates to the Police Civil Service Commission.  Mr. Segrave 
moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body  present with the exception of  Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral 
appointment). 
 
 Report of Community Facility Fees for Fiscal Year 2020-2021 pursuant to Section 4.2.5 of 
the Unified Developmen t Code (UDC).  Charles Bloom , Planning  and Development Director, 
provided information on the ann ual report which included  the source of  fees collected by 
subdivision/development, disbursement of fees, and balance in fee accounts as of June 30 th.  Mr. 
Bloom noted the total fee collection of $495,044.00 and expenditures in the amount of $115,763.00 
for fiscal year 2021 leaving a total ending fund balance of $979,347.00.  Comments were made 
relating to positive efforts in collection of  outstanding fees and appreciation was expressed to 
previous governing bodies for their foresight in creation of the fee process.   Mr. Segrave moved 
to acknowledge receipt of the report, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. 
    
 Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held October 25, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Penstemon 
Business LLC d/b/a Bella Fuoco, 2115 Warren Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
  
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. White expressed appreciation to Jennifer McClelland, Executive Assistant to the City 
Council, and members of  Information Technology  for their efforts involving acquiring and 
installation of technical equipment in Council Chambers.  Mayor Collins echoed Mr. White ’s 
comments and expressed appreciation to Andrew Hays, Civic Center , for his involvement in the 
process.      
 
 Dr. Aldrich expressed appreciation to Sgt. Mark Solberg, Cheyenne Police Department, 
for his recent presentation involving property crimes in the city.   
 
 Dr. Aldrich announced City Council Ward III will be holding a Council and Coffee event 
on Saturday, October 16th from 9 a.m. to 11 a.m. at Dazbog Coffee (3911 E. Pershing Blvd.).      
 
 Mr. Johnson expressed appreciation to fellow council members on their collective efforts 
in accomplishing many of their goals for this year.   
 
 Mr. Laybourn provided comments regarding the Consent Agenda process, the importance 
of public involvement and his desire to thoroughly provide funding information to the public.    
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:05 p.m.   
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kylie N. Soden,   
Deputy City Clerk, CMC 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  October 20, 2021
