MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-10-25 | CLIP_ID: 822 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 822

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 25, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel (via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom 
Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was 
recited.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by Dr. Aldrich , and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. White, agenda i tem #20(b) was removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. Segrave m oved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr.  White.   Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 11, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Penstemon Business LLC d/b/a Bella Fuoco, 2115 Warren Ave.,  
Cheyenne, WY 82001.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones explained the business currently holds 
a restaurant license but due to new owners a new license was applied for. There being no further 
comments or input, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to Finance 
Committee. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020(B), Members -Terms-
Vacancies, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming  (Postponed from October 11, 2021) . 
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Stefanie Boster explained a memorandum 
was presented to members of the governing body that advised the proposed ordinance amendment 
brings the city into compliance with state statute .   Ms. Boster confirmed the ordinance clarifies  
who may appoint the council representative to the Downtown Development Authority Board 
(DDA).  Ms. Boster explained that state statue implies either the Mayor or governing body can 
make the council representative appointment and the language in the statute does not use the term 
“Council President” and that it  refers to the governing body .  Ms. Boost er advised her 
interpretation of the statute is the legislative intent was for the governing body to make the decision 
and that the appointment could be made by the Mayor or Council President.  Comments were made 
that the DDA Board is one of 22 boards wher e the Council President has typically made the 
appointment of the respective Council Representative and that only a few require governing body 
approval.  Discussion continued on history of the board  noting the council member a ppointee is 
usually a long -term appointment  and City Code Section 2.76.020 relating to th e council 
representative appointment and the reorganization of DDA Board Authority.  Continued support 
and opposition were voiced by members of the governing body citing state statute, city code an d 
past practices, and general desire for consistency across the board.  It was noted that sections (a) 
and (b) in state statute 15 -9-205 appear to be conflicting relating to this particular council 
representative board appointment.  Motion failed due to a tie vote on 3rd and final reading. Voting 
“yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” - Dr. Aldrich, 
Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Collins, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified D evelopment Code (UDC) to 
clarify the interpretation of Lots and amend Accessory Building Regulations.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by 
substitute dated October 18, 2021, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Connor White, 
Planning and Development, explained the substitute noting there is a text change from “through 
lot” to “double fronted lot” to match the rest of the ordinance.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4398) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
modifying the Density Standards of UD C 5.1.5D “Multi-Dwelling Buildings (Apartment)” Lot 
Types.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4399) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
modify the MUR, MUB, and MUE zone districts’ intent, context, and transitions .  Dr. Rinne  
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4400) 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 10.04.110, Amnesty Program, of 
Chapter 10.04, General Provisions, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich .  Upon inquiry,  Deputy City Attorney Alessandra McCoy 
Fakelman, explained she is working to understand the parking program and is looking to introduce 
a companion resolution in the near future.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama  Drive.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend 
by substitute dated October 19, 2021, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mayor Collins advised the 
substitute is due to the county changing their zoning, therefore, correcting our document.  Motion 
to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended 
by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-Density Residential for land located southwest of t he intersection of Braehill Road, 
Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated October 19, 2021, seconded by Mr.  
Segrave.  Mayor Collins advised the  substitute is due to the county changing their zoning, 
therefore, correcting our document.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.   
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
modify Articles 2.1 and 2.2 regarding posted and published notice requirements for divisions of 
land and zoning applications – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 15.08.031, Building Permit Fees -
Exceptions for Incentivization Program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, 
of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City o f Cheyenne, Wyoming, to 
reinstate the City of Cheyenne Core Development Incentivization Program – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. 
White) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Water 
Development Commission for grant funding for the Crystal Bypass Pipeline Project in the amount 
of $2,546,000.00 and authorizing the Director of the Board of Public Utilities to execute the 
application and all related documents.  Frank Strong, Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities (BOPU), 
provided information on the grant and project stating  the resolution and following resolution are 
for the BOPU to apply for funding for the Crystal Bypass and noted the project will allow for water 
to be bypassed to allow for maintenance.   Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. 
Comments were made related to applying for 2 separate grants and 2 different funding amount 
requests for the project and request was made for a review of how SLIB grants have been handled 
in the past.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Strong explained most reservoirs have the capability for different 
water paths, but Crystal Reservoir does not have th e capability.  Appreciatio n was expressed to 
BOPU for their forward thinking related to the project.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6182)   
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming State Loan 
and Investment Board for a loan from the Wyoming Drinking Water State Revolving Fund for the 
Crystal Bypass Pipeline Project in the amount of $1,254,000.00 and authorizing the Director of 
the Board of Public Utilities to execute the application and all related  documents.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#6183) 
 
 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for the owner of the 
property located at 2621 Granite Peak Drive to allow installation of on-site sewage holding tanks 
(septic system) located within the City.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6184) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for the owner of the 
property lo cated at 8910 Hutchins Drive to allow installation of on -site sewage holding tanks 
(septic system) located within the City.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6185) 
 
 Human Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and United Way of Laramie 
County to provide services to various community programs (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) .  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve in an amount of $500,000.00, seconded by  Mr. White.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to amend by substitute dated Oc tober 21, 2021, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Deputy City 
Attorney Alessandra McCoy Fakelman advise d the substitute contains a name change of one of 
the legal entities from Peak Wellness (Mental Health Services) to Volunteers of American 
Northern Rockies.  Vicki Nemecek , Director of Public W orks, ad ded the agreement has been 
changed from a 2 year contract to a 1 year contract.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Rod Hottle, United Way, provided information on 
the following: request for 1 year contract and future request for 3 year contracts for the purpose of 
budgeting for the receiving agencies and for data analysis of the program, new application process 
in the future that will go out to the community for funding availability , explanation that United 
Way is vetting the application process for agencies and they are looking at a common agenda that 
looks at many different aspects and that will come back before the governing body for approval , 
past meetings held  in 2020-2021 and participation of the council representative on the Human 
Services Advisory Council, influence of  Human Services Advisory Council in determining 
funding awards, how funding from the City in the amount of $10,500.00  is used which includes 
continuation of collecting reports, administration of funding, program data analysis and end of 
year reporting and the outside auditing agency that performs the annual independent audit which 
has not resulted in any issues.    Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised prior to providing funding to 
the United Way to manage payment of funding awards, the city’s function was to complete 
requisitions for payment to the agencies.  It was noted that Laramie County also makes payment 
to United Way to manage the funding awards .  Ms. Nemecek explained the H uman Services 
Advisory Council has existed for over a decade  and t he resolution is created every 4 year s 
according to the 1% Sales Tax cycle.  Mr. Hottle provided information on the process for agencies 
who are not chosen to receiv e funding and noted  assistance is provided for future application 
opportunities and other ideas are vetted for optional funding resources.  Mr. Hottle explained upon 
inquiry that 100% of the $ 250,00.00 in funding goes to the agencies.  Request was made to 
recirculate the annual report to members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7434) 
 
 Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of Transportation 
(WYDOT) for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Grant to assist in providing a local match 
for operating funds for the Transit Program for Fiscal Year 2022 .  Mr. Roybal advised the 
recommendation of the Finance Committee was to approve in an amount of $282,885.00, however, 
it has come to the committee’s attention that matching funds are not required, therefore, the amount 
of the grant award is $160,000.00.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Upon 
inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, advised the city receives $160,000.00 and that 
a match hasn’t been required since 2009.  Ms. Nemecek further noted that the city has requested 
WYDOT to change the agreement language, but it is the standard language and is what WYDOT 
has chosen to continue to utilize.  Ms. Nemecek noted Casper and Cheyenne don’t have to provide 
matching funds  and the city  has received a letter i ndicating that a match does not have to be 
provided.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7435)  
 
[CA] 2021-2022 Collective Labor A greement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and 
Cheyenne Firefighters, Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters .  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7436) 
 
[CA] Grant Award A greement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming  Office of 
Homeland Security for the purchase of two complete bomb suits – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Grant Award A greement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming  Office of 
Homeland Security for the purchase of two Recon Robot Throwbot 2 – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
              
[CA] Telecommunications Right-of-Way and Use Agreement between Uniti National LLC and 
the City of Cheyenne – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for the 
housing of juvenile detainees in the Laramie County Juvenile Services Center located at the Archer 
Complex – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Grant Award Agreement with the State of Wyoming Office of Homeland Security for a 
grant from the U.S. Department of Homeland Security (DHS), F EMA, Grant Programs 
Directorate, SHSP Grant Fiscal Year 2021 in the amount of $106,817.35 for the Cheyenne 
Regional Response Team #7 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Tourism Promotion 
Joint Powers Board, d/b/a Visit Cheyenne regarding the partnership to develop planning and 
design and implementation for a first phase of the Belvoir Ranch/Big Hole Project – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Robert Chamberlin to 
authorize filing of platting applications(s) for City -owned lands for the purpose of access and 
future development – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Subgrantee Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for the 2021 
Byrne Justice Assistance Grant for community -oriented policing overtime and benefits and 
firearms accessories (JAG21 Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Subgrantee Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for FY 2021 
Law Enforcement Mental Health and Wellness Act Grant (LEMHWA Grant) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Lease Agreement between the State of Wyoming, Department of Wyoming State Parks 
and Cultural Resources, Division of State Parks, Historic Sites and Trails and the City of Cheyenne 
for lands in Curt Gowdy State Park – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne a nd HDR Engineering, 
Inc., for the 5 th Street Bridge Over Crow Creek Design Project (2019 -2022 1% Sales Tax) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7287 between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne 
Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO), and Benchmark Engineers, PC, Cheyenne, for 
additional professional services for the Walterscheid Boulevard Reconstruction Plan (MPO 
Federal Funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $9,035.00). 
(#7287) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7286 between the City of Cheyenne and Summit 
Engineering for additional construction management services for the Construction Management 
Services for Multi -Use Underpass for Pedestrian and Bicycles on College Drive connecting the 
Laramie County Community College and Sweetgrass Development (LCCC Greenway Underpass 
Project) (6th Penny Greenway Funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6959 between the City of Cheyenne and Short Elliot 
Hendrickson, Inc., for additional engineering and construction management services for the 
Airport Parkway Pavement Rehabilitation Project (2018-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6606 between the City of Cheyenne and Trihydro 
Corporation for additional ground and surface water sampling at the Snyder -Ames Pumphouse 
Property (2018-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Bijou, Inc. d/b/a 
Capitol Cinema 16, 3410 Concord Rd., Cheyenne, WY 82001.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 8, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD#1 Board Room) , 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by 
Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located north of the intersection of Old Happy Jack 
Road and Westland Road. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Johnson requested information on when updates are made to the website and online 
code with regards to ordinance changes.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, 
advised UDC changes are typically added to the website within a week.  City Clerk  Kris Jones 
provided information on submitting information to the online code company noting there is a 
delayed process as the ordinances are codified through a third party company.  
 
Mr. White expressed appreciation to th e new Fire Union members, the City Attorney’s 
office and Fire Department Chief Kopper for their collaborative efforts working towards the 
approval of the Collective Labor Agreement.  
 
 Appreciation was expressed to city staff members Jennifer McClelland, M ichael Skinner 
and Kris Jones for their assistance in determining the LCSD #1 board room me ets the needs for 
the council meeting. 
 
 Dr. Aldrich  expressed appreciation to the Public Works Department for their quick 
assistance with a recent land ownership review and mowing and a stop sign repair. 
 
 Mr. Laybourn noted that his elected official career began  52 years ago today  in the same 
meeting room (LCSD #1 Board Room) the council meeting was being held in. 
 
 Mr. Johnson requested citizens to reach out to members of the governing body and city 
officials if they have any questions related to how city tax dollars are being utilized, noting a recent 
incorrect reference to an electric scooter operation being paid for by city tax dollars. 
 
 Dr. Aldrich provided information on the upcoming 2021 Sixth Penny election noting the 
importance of voting on proposition projects. 
 
Mayor Collins requested the governing body meet in executive session to discuss a 
proposed litigation to which the governing body may be a party.   Mr. White moved to meet in 
executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16 -4-405(a)(iii), to consider a matter involving 
proposed litigation to which the governing body may be a party, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mayor Collins announced members 
would meet in executive session, not open to the public; requested the  City Attorney, City Clerk, 
and Brad Brooks, Director of the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU)  be in attendance  and stated 
there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive session, and that 
the regular City Council meeting would offic ially conclude at the close of the executive session. 
(Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 7:17 p.m.).     
 
 Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 8:21p.m.   
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:21p.m.   
 
 
 
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk

  November 5, 2021

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-11-08 | CLIP_ID: 824 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 824

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 8, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel (via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom 
Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was 
recited and led by Boy Scout Troop 114.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by  Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson  and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item  #18(f) was removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. White moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 25, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for land located north of the intersection 
of Old Happy Jack Road and Westland Road .  Mark Christensen, Planning and Development 
Department, explained they received a petition to annex the land  from the property owners , 
provided information on the property  and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation 
conditions contained in state statutes.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Christensen advised the property will be 
utilized as an automobile shop with window tinting services and that the Board of Public Utilities 
deemed the proposed annexation area suitable for utilizing water and sewer in the area.  There 
being no further comments or input, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 10.04.110, Amnesty Program, of 
Chapter 10.04, General Provisions, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Comments were made related to resolutions that will 
accompany the ordinance in the future establishing periodic one-time amnesty programs.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4401) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama  Drive.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#4402) 
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-Density Residential for land located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, 
Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on  third and final  reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#4403) 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
modify Articles 2.1 and 2.2 regarding posted and published notice requirements for divisions of 
land and zoning applications .  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  
Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Mark Christensen, Planning and Development Department, explained that 
generally the proposed ordinance amendment outlines the new requirements for post ing signage 
notices for applications submitted to the Planning and Development Department, removing the 
requirement to post new notices after postponements, clarifies the posted notice requirement for 
city-initiated zoning applications and requires the city to mail notice to the affected property 
owners for city-initiated zoning applications.  Mr. Christensen noted the proposed amendment was 
brought forward due to staff concerns with current procedures.   Upon further inquiry, Mr. 
Christensen explained the parameter for mailing notices is currently properties within 300 ft. of 
the subject property.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 15.08.031, Building Permit Fees -
Exceptions for Incentivization Program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, 
of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to 
reinstate the City of Cheyenne Core Development Incentivization Program.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Information on the incentivization program 
was provided including the affected area, previous program  restrictive timeframe and desire to 
reinstate the program, how the  program will allow infill development downtown and anticipated 
funding in the amount of $50 million for downtown projects.  Comments and concerns were made 
related to the eligible proposed area being only for the downtown core area.  Request was made 
that should an amendment be made to include Wards II and III, the amendment be vetted through 
the City Attorney’s office.  Further discussion included: contribution to economic development, 
infill development, available private project funding resources, project incentivization, benefits to 
small businesses, remediation costs , and inclusion of big box developments and franchise 
agreements if the program were to encompass other areas of town, success  with prior program, 
Planning Commission involvement with the program, general effective dates of ordinances, 
support and opposition to including other areas of town in the program at this time, importance of 
a vibrant and inviting downtown and types of pr ojects that may be eligible to participate in the 
program.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.   
 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northeast of the intersection of Old Happy Jack Road and Westland Road – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of

for land located northeast of the intersection of Old Happy Jack Road and Westland Road –

 ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of

[CA] Grant Award A greement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming  Office of 
Homeland Security for the purchase of two complete bomb suits .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (in an amount of $71,318.00). (#7438) 
 
 Grant Award A greement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming  Office of 
Homeland Security for the pu rchase of two Recon Robot Throwbot 2 . Captain David Janes, 
Cheyenne Police Department, presented a robot device for reference and provided information on 
recon robots and  the grant stating the robots are  utilized by being placed in compromising 
locations, therefore protecting human lives.  He further provided information on how the use of 
the robots applies to the 4th Amendment, noting case law exists, and advised a robot is an extension 
of an officer.  Captain Janes explained policies that must be followed to utilize the robots.  Upon 
inquiry, Captain Janes stated the current robot has been in use for 10 years and needs to be replaced.  
He confirmed the company that provides the robots is reputable and provides maintenance services 
noting the biggest difference in the new model is  it will provide audio in addition to video.   
Comments were made related to case law, search and seizure and support of utilizing the robots 
while following policies.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an amount of $34,978.64, seconded by 
Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7439) 
 
 Telecommunications Right-of-Way and Use Agreement between Uniti National, LLC and 
the City of Cheyenne .  Mr. Roybal advised at the request of staff, the item died due to a lack of 
motion at the Finance Committee, therefore, there was no recommendation from the committee.   
 
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for the 
housing of juvenile detainees in the Laramie County Juvenile Services Center located at the Archer 
Complex.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7440) 
 
[CA] Grant Award Agreement with the State of Wyoming Office of Homeland Security for a 
grant from the U.S. Department of Homeland Security (DHS), FEMA, Grant Programs 
Directorate, SHSP Grant Fiscal Year 2021 in the amount of $106,817.35 for the Cheyen ne 
Regional Response Team #7.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7441) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Tourism Promotion 
Joint Powers Board, d/b/a Visit Cheyenne regarding the partnership to develop planning and 
design and implementation for a first phase of the Belvoir Ranch/Big Hole Project .  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve  in the amount of  $100,000.00, seconded by Mr.  White.  Upon inquiry, City 
Treasurer Robin Lockman advised the funding has not been reappropriated, noting the funding is 
currently in the Annexation Improvement Fund and has been there for some time.  Comments were 
made and included: support of the  project and the importance of public involvement , project 
funding source and desire to move the project forward in a timely manner.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7442) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Robert Chamberlin to 
authorize filing of pl atting applications(s) for City -owned lands for the purpose of access and 
future development.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Upon inquiry, Mark 
Christensen, Planning & Development Department, noted the application is for the platting of the 
property and to allow the applicant to plat the property and reported the Urban Renewal Authority 
(URA) recently met and heard the application and development agreement between the city and 
Swagger Construction, LLC and the URA recommended approval in an amount not to exceed $1.2 
million with tax increment financing.   Mr. Christensen provided further information on platting 
and replatting processes for city owned land including land donated to the city.  He advised not all 
land owned by the city will be zoned P -Public d ue to  different use types.  Mr. Christensen 
explained when the land located adjacent to the Ice & Event s Center was donated to the city, it 
was done through a deeding process.  He further reported that a platting process clarifies the legal 
description for the title and the Laramie County A ssessor’s Office.  It was noted that for an  
administrative plat, the applicant would need to submit a preliminary plat application for approval.  
Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised the Ice & Events Center was originally zoned CB -
Community Business as it was started as a private business.  Mayor Collins advised some inquiries 
and comments being made were not germane to the topic.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#7443)

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-3-22 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 1 ton, regular cab, full 
size pickup truck, 4x4, with an 8-foot box and a fleet work truck package for the City of Cheyenne, 
Community Recreation & Events Department -Weed & Pest Division (We ed & Pest Fund) – 
referred to Finance Committee.      
                
 [CA] Consideration of Bid #E-4-22 for furnishing two (2) new 2021 -2022, 11 feet wide diesel 
rotary mowers for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events D epartment-Parks 
Division (2015-2018 and 2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.

 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Notice of Emergency Procurement.  In accordance with the City of Cheyenne Purchasing 
Policy, and Emergency Procurement has been authorize d by Mayor Collins for the emergency 
Municipal Building HVAC System Cleaning.  The emergency cleaning has been authorized to 
Mighty Ducts for $42,143.00. 
 
 Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held November 22, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCS D#1 Board Room) , 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for a limited retail liquor license transfer of location application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for AmVets Post 10, from 3839 E. Lincolnway to 1820 Ridge Rd., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
  Mayor Collins reported on a request to rename a triangle shaped area of land near Randall 
Ave. after Bill Dubois in which he has received a name change petition containing 594 signatures 
that will be submitted to the City Clerk for processing.  Mayor Collins advised members of the 
governing body the petition is available for them to sign if they so choose.   
 
 Mr. Johnson noted he believes comments and inquiries made during discussion relating to 
the Memorandum of Understanding between the City and Robert Chamberlin were relevant to the 
topic. 
   
 Mr. Johnson provided information on Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board Christmas 
ornament sales that benefit the airport fountain restoration project, noting any donations for the 
project would be appreciated. 
 
 Mr. Johnson commented on  a recent McCormick Jr. High student fatality and offered 
contact information relating to Laramie County School District #1’s policies regarding 
management of school crosswalks and correlation with the City’s Engineering Department.     
 
 Dr. Aldrich recognized city employees who have  served in the military  noting the city’s 
recognition of the Veteran’s Day holiday.  Dr. Aldrich further stated the city and community  are 
heavily indebted to our local military personnel. 
 
 Mr. White expressed appreciation to military veterans and commented on the student 
fatality expressing sympathy to their family and friends and reported he was made aware of 2 more 
students who were struck  in a cross walk .  He stressed that public safety involves the entire  
community and asked for extra caution around school areas.     
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:04 p.m.   
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2021-11-22 | CLIP_ID: 826 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 826

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 22, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and 
Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.   
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the des ignation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by  Mr. Roybal, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. White and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item  #19(c) was removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Upon request by  Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. 
Laybourn, agenda item  #19(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. White m oved to 
approve remaining items, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 8, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a limited retail liquor license transfer of location 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for AmVets Post 10, from 3839 E. Lincolnway to 
1820 Ridge Rd., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Upon inquiry, Kristina F. Jones , City Clerk, advised the 
applicant was present and available for question s.  Ms. Jones further advised the transfer was 
requested as the business is changing their physical location.  There being no further comments or 
input, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee.   
 
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
modify Articles 2.1 and 2.2 regarding posted and published notice requirements for divisions of 
land and zoning applications.   Reporting for  Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4404) 
  
 ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Section 15.08.031, Building Permit Fees -
Exceptions for Incentivization Program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, 
of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to 
reinstate the City of Cheyenne Core Development Incentivization Program.  Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated November 15, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. 
Roybal explained that although the Finance Committee recommended approving a substitute 
ordinance dated November 15, 202 1, staff recommends defeating that substitute and i nstead 
approving a revised substitute ordinance dated November 22, 2021.  Motion to amend failed.  
Voting ‘no” – all members of the governing body .  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute 
dated November 22, 2021, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  It was noted the s ubstitute clarifies that the 
Planning & Development Department will be responsible for project management.  Comments and 
concerns were made related to the eligible proposed area being only for the downtown core area  
and sunset clause.  Discussion included ability to amend the ordinance in the future, if necessary, 
after sufficient data is reviewed.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
(#4405) 
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northeast of the intersection of Old Happy Jack Road and Westland Road .  D r. Rinne 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.   Motion carried.   Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body.     
 
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County LI Light Industrial to LI Light Industrial 
for land located northeast of the intersection of Old Happy Jack Road and Westland Road .  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.   Motion carried.   Votin g 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.     
  
 ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from HR High -Density Residential to CB 
Community Business for land located southeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Western Hills 
Boulevard and Osage Avenue .  Mr. Segrave declared a conflict of interest due to a business 
partnership with the developer.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. 
Aldrich.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Cook who voted “no” and Mr. Segrave who did not vote.         
    
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a final plat for 
Meadowlark Estates, 2 nd Filing, a replat of Tract 1 and a portion of Tracts 2 -9 of Meadowlark 
Estates, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of and adjacent to the 
intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road) .  Trey Rinne, AVI, agent for the 
applicant, made himself available for questions.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt with the condition that 
the final plat would not be signed until the development agreement has been approved by the 
governing body, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#6186) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City Treasurer to discharge uncollectible debts pursuant 
to Wyoming Statute §16-4-502.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6187) 
 
 
 
 RESOLUTION – Certifying the Converse Avenue Reconstruction Plan.  Dr. Rinne moved 
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Rande Pouppirt, area business/property owner, requested 
consideration of approving the alternate design which would not affect the current number of his 
parking spaces, noting the preferred design would remove one half of his available parking.  MPO 
Director Tom Mason provided details on the design process and explained options will be 
reviewed by the City Engineer prior to implementation.  Upon inquiry, Tom Cobb, City Engineer, 
explained a consultant will be obtained for review of design options which will include the entire 
intersection, there will be  public hearings throughout the process for public vetting and that the 
final design does not typically come back for gove rning body approval.  Following request by 
members of the governing body, Mr. Cobb committed to bringing the final design options before 
the governing body prior to implementation.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the  exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  
(#6188)  
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for a Land and Water 
Conservation Fund Grant for a restroom facility in Saddle Ridge Park  – referred to Finance 
Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)

 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Plan One Architects, 
for the Level I/Level II Feasibility Study for 15 th Street Corridor Plan (Annexation Improvement 
Fund).  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $100,000.00, seconded by Mr. 
White.   Dr. Rinne moved to amend by deleting Reed Ave nue funds from Exhibit A -Scope of 
Work, Section (l), seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all members

were made regarding use of 1% S pecific Purpose Tax funding and overages, funding sources 
available for the project and opposition to allocation of 2017 Reed Avenue Corridor tax funds.  
Discussion included review of the 2017 1% Specific Purpose Tax ballot projects, faith of the voters

excluding the use of 2017 Specific Purpose Tax funds for Reed Avenue in Exhibit A -Scope of 
Work Section (l), seconded by Mr. White.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members

 Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Swagger Construction, LLC, 
for Tax Increment Financing for the Hitching Post Plaza (TIF Funding).  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $1,200,000.00, seconded by Mr. White.  City Att orney

seconded by Mr. Johnson.   Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Main motion as amended carried.   V oting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

Notice of Emergency Procurement.  In accordance with the City of Cheyenne  Purchasing 
Policy, an Emergency Procurement has been authorized by Mayor Collins for the purchase of a 
replacement lighting console for the Cheyenne Civic Cent er, as the existing console has failed 
beyond repair.  The emergency procurement has been authorized to Brown Note Productions for 
$51,097.19.      
 
 Under other business:   
 
 Upon inquiry, Community Recreation & Events Director Teresa Moore provided details 
relating to the necessity of the Emergency Procurement.  Ms. Moore advised the lighting console 
failed beyond repair and due to previously schedule events at the Civic Center, it became necessary 
to replace the equipment to meet the requirements of those event contracts.  Ms. Moore reported 
on funding sources used for the purchase which include funds from fundraising and Shutter Venues 
funding, noting they did solicit quotes.  Purchasing Manager TJ Barttelbort provided information 
on the normal bidding process and associated timelines.  It was further noted that expenses 
associated with an option of renting the necessary equipment would have become more expensive 
than the emergency purchase. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:16 p.m.   
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  December 3, 2021

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
December 13, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.   
Absent: Ken Esquibel.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was 
recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Dr. Aldrich moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

(per Consent Agenda) carried.

for land located northeast of the intersection of Old Happy Jack Road and Westland Road .  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave. Comments were 
made in support of the zone change and how it will help improve blight in the area and will benefit 
Crow Creek and expansion of the Greenway.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4407)

Community Business for land located southeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Western Hills 
Boulevard and Osage Avenue .  Mr. Segrave declared a conflict of interest due to a business 
partnership with the applicant.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded 
by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with 
the exception of Mr. Segrave who did not vote. (#4408) 
 
         
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create the definitions, ‘Functional Turf’ and ‘Non -Functional Turf’ and

ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Ordinance No. 4396 Defining the Terms and

2021-2022 – referred to Finance Committee.  (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, 
Mr. Roybal and Mr. White)  
      
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for a Land and Water 
Conservation Fund Grant for a restroom facility in Saddle Ridge Park .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried.  (#6189)    
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant application under the Assistance to Firefighters Grant Program, on behalf of 
the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of purchasing new vehicle exhaust 
systems for existing fire stat ion infrastructure.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal 
advised that at the request of staff and due to the ineligibility of funding for this type of equipment 
under a regional grant, the item died due to the lack of a motion, therefore, there is not a 
recommendation on the item.  Item died due to lack of a motion. 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant application under the Assistance to Firefighters Grant Program, on behalf of 
the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of purchasing a new medium rescue 
vehicle and Rapid Intervention Team training and program development .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6190) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Designating the allocation of WyoLo tto Revenues to the City of 
Cheyenne be distributed to Community Recreation and Events for the purpose of construction, 
remediation, and completion of quality of life projects in the City of Cheyenne for a five (5) year 
period.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by 
substitute dated December 13, 2021 which amends the 8th “Whereas” clause by striking “following 
the first quarterly receipt of WyoLotto revenues to the City in 2022” and replace s it with “July 1, 
2022”, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  It was explained the reason for the amendment is for alignment 
with the FY 2022 budget as the revenue has already been budgeted.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. 
Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.   Comments were made on the following topics: 2021 goal 
setting session not aligning with withdrawing funds from the general fund for the resolution, 
perceived poor timing of resolution and current budgetary concerns  relating to the Fire 
Department’s overtime and payroll expenses, proposed employee raises and reinstated longevity, 
support for Community Recreation and Events including parks and quality of life projects, concern 
of using funding from the general fund for Community Recreation and Events and request to 
consider other funding sources for the resolution. Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Robin Lockman 
confirmed the amendment will utilize funding out of the 2 023 FY budget. An explanation was 
provided by Mr. Johnson, sponsor of the resolution, noting the resolution was  brought forward 
after the 6 th penny election and that Community Recreation and Events historically has had the 
most cuts for projects and they typically have had low priority in project funding.  Mr. Johnson 
commented on the Great Outdoors Colorado Parks projects, which the resolution was modeled 
after, and advised park related maintenance is often delayed due to lack of funding, noting  the 
funds can be used to improve existing parks infrastructure.  Further comments were made related 
to budget cuts regularly effecting Community Recreation and Events , the importance of 
maintaining and improving our parks, use of general fund dollars, success of 6 th penny election 
which will free up funding that would have been used for the airport, replacing revenue for the 
resolution, concern of “earmarking” general fund dollars, importance of managing the City budget 
during difficult times, instances where funds were earmarked in the budget including Windfarm 
dollars (contractual agreement) and sanitation funds and the possibility of delaying the resolution 
until it is determined how the budget will look for next fiscal year .  Upon inquiry, Ms. Lockman 
provided budget information noting the total budget for the current fiscal year is $152,183,794 
with approximately $55 million in the general fund.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the exception of Dr.  Aldrich, Mr. 
Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#6191)

not to exceed $68,541.00). (#7456)

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
                
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Christmas holiday schedule, the regularly 
scheduled City Council meeting on Monday, December 27, 2021, will instead be held on Tuesday, 
December 28, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. at  Storey Gym (LCSD#1 Board Room) , 2811 House Avenue, 
Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting. 
 
 Notice of Emergency Procurement . In accordance with the City of Cheyenne Purchasing 
Policy, an Emergency Procurement has been authorized by Mayor Collins for the emergency 
Municipal Building HVAC Duct Sealing.  The emergency duct sealing has been authorized to 
Mighty Ducts for $71,240.00.  
 
 Under other business:   
 
 Mayor Collins announced the recent birth of Councilman Ken Esquibel’s granddaughter. 
 
 Mr. Johnson provided information on an upcoming  City Council Ward III Council and 
Coffee event on Saturday, December 18th from 9 am - 11 am at Dazbog Coffee (3911 E. Pershing 
Blvd.).  Mr. Johnson further noted sales of the Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board Christmas 
ornaments will follow the Council and Coffee event at the Depot.  
 
  Mr. White moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16 -4-
405(a)(iii) and §16-4-405(a)(viii), seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present.  Mayor Collins advised the executive session will be held 
to discuss potential l itigation and labor issues and  announced members would meet in executive 
session, not open to the public; requested the  City Attorney, City Clerk, City Treasurer and Fire 
Chief be in attendance and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result 
of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at 
the close of the executive session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 6:46 p.m.).     
 
 Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 7:52 p.m. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:52 p.m.   
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
December 28, 2021 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich  (via Zoom), 
Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom 
Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance 
was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed wi th the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by  Mr. White, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Segrave and Mr. Cook, agenda item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Segrave, 
agenda item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda .  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and 
pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Cook, agenda item #28(b) was removed from 
the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Segrave  moved to approve  remaining items, seconded by Mr. White. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 13, 2021.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create the definitions, ‘Functional Turf’ and ‘Non -Functional Turf’ and 
amending various sections of Article 6 .  Dale Spofield spoke in favor of the ordinance noting 
several benefits including water conservation during drought seasons.  Mr. Spofield provided 
information on ways he has changed the landscaping at his residence which has resulted in water 
conservation.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved  to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Comment was made that the governing body has gone to great 
lengths to update commercial landscaping requirements in the last few years.  Upon inquiry, Jeanie 
Vetter, Greenway and Parks Planner, Planning and Development Department, provided lot square 
footage and water usage information for the Maverik gas station property located at 140 Gardenia 
Dr. compared to Panera Bread which is  similar in lot size.  Ms. Vetter noted that Panera Bread 
uses substantially less water than Maverik due to having no turf grass but still meeting current 
landscaping requirements.  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development  Department, explained upon 
inquiry that 75 site plan applications had been submitted in 2021.  Ms. Vetter further advised that 
the ordinance amendment would likely result in saving millions of gallons of water per year based 
on the amount of site plans submitted in 2021. Comments  were made on the following topics:  
updating the watering process at golf courses whi ch could save 195 million gallons of water 
annually, water reservoirs being at 89% capacity at the end of the watering season and are currently 
at full capacity, amount of water savings possible by eliminating outside user agreements and the 
completion of the southside water pipe project which will result in generating more water. Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
        
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development – 
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD - Planned Unit Development District of the Unified Development Code, Amending 
Ordinance No. 4232 by modifying certain standards of the Adopted District, Pl anned Unit 
Development (PUD), for a portion of Sections 8 & 9, all of the W1/2 Section 14, a portion of the 
E ½ E ½ of Section 17, and all of Sections 15, 16, & 21; Township 13 North, Range 66 West of 
the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming  (Located to the southeast of the intersection of E. College 
Drive and Avenue C) .  Dr. Rinne advised there was no recommendation from Public Services 
Committee.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve  on second reading  with staff conditions #1 & #2, 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  It was noted the developer is requesting to build 30 complexes containing 
120 units that would be 40% contiguous to the Greenway and the development wouldn’t include 
a clubhouse.  Dr. Rinne explained  proposed staff condition #1 is for the units to rem ain at 100 
instead of 120 and it appears the applicant is amenable to the condition.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  
        
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Ordinance No. 4396 Defining the Terms and 
Conditions of Employment for Sworn Officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 
2021-2022.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 10.76, Abandoned Vehicles, of Title 
10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal 
and Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 8.60, Nuisances, of Title 8, Health 
and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.  Roybal and Mr. 
White) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 6.08.200, Keeping of Bees, of Chapter 
6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, 
Mr. Roybal and Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Cheyenne 
Ice and Events Center, 4 th Filing, a replat of Lots 1 and 2, P.A.S. Subdivision 1 st Filing and a 
Portion of Lot 1, Block 1 Back 40 Subdivision First Filing situated in a portion of Section 1, 
Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming 
(located northwest of the intersection of West Lincolnway and Stinson Avenue) .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6192)           
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Kornegay 
Homesites, 3rd Filing, a replat of Lots 8 through 12, inclusive, and 8’alley directly north of and 
adjacent to Lots 8 through 12, inclusive, Kornegay Homesites, lying within the northwest ¼ of 
Section 19, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6 th P.M., City of Cheyenn e, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of Quincy Road and Yellowstone Road).  
Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute map 
dated September 15, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mayor Collins noted the substitute map 
contains updated ownership information .  Motion to amend by substitute map carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6193) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Whitney 
Ranch, 5th Filing, a replat of Lot 14, Block 9 of Whitney Ranch 1 st Filing situated in a portion of 
Section 23, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6 th P.M., City o f Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Dell Range Blvd and Gysel Place) .  
Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute 
map dated December 14, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “ yes” – all members 
of the governing body. (#6194)  
 
 RESOLUTION – Designating local banks as depositories for Municipal Funds .  Mayor 
Collins declared a conflict of interest due to a business affiliation, relinquished duties of the Chair 
to Council President White and vacated the meeting room during voting.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mayor Collins who didn’t vote.  President White relinquished duties 
of the Chair back to Mayor Collins following the vote. (#6195) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for the Community 
Development Block Grant for a covered picnic structure at Pioneer Park .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6196)          
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Reinstating longevity pay for eligible City of Cheyenne non -uniformed 
employees.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6197) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing a salary increase of one dollar and fifty cents ($1.50) per 
hour to eligible employees of the City of Cheyenne . Seth Lloyd, Cheyenne Public Employees 
Association, spoke in favor of the resolution and against exempting otherwise eligible employees 
who have received a pay increase since July 1, 2021.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by 
Mr. White.  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Robin Lockman advised that due to higher than projected 
revenues and reserves and anticipated continued economic progress, the pay raise and 
reinstatement of longevity pay is possible.  Mayor Collins noted it is believed revenues will 
continue to increase supporting the raises and that the $1. 50 per hour raise is desired to be most 
beneficial for all employees across the pay scale.  It was explained  the resolution is specifically 
for pay increases for eligible employees and longevity pay reinstatement was addressed through 
other agenda items.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6198)              
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant application under the Assistance to Firefighters Grant Program, on behalf of 
the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of purchasing new lift air bag kit .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6199) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant application under the Assistance to Firefighters Grant Program, on behalf of 
the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of purchasing new fire hose and 
nozzles.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6200)     
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City Treasurer to allocate Revenue Recovery Funds for 
identified purposes from the U.S. Department of Treasury for the American Rescue Plan 
Coronavirus State and Local Fiscal Recovery Funds in the amount of $986,645.00 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6201) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Proposing the City of Cheyenne to become the ‘Climate Wellness 
Capitol’ of the world by working with the Carbon Asset Network for the benefit of soil health and 
soil organic carbon capture.  Dale Steenbergen, President/CEO of the Greater Cheyenne Chamber 
of Commerce, presented and read into the record a letter of support from the Wyoming Stock 
Growers Association (WSGA) for the proposed resolution.  Mr. Steenbergen provided information 
on carbon credits and block chain noting the resolution will bring the 2 components together  and 
provide a path forward.  Upon inquiry regarding language in the resolution relating to certification 
by the City of Cheyenne, Mr. Steenbergen explained the City will only certify that the Carbon 
Asset Network has completed certain work.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Steenbergen advised that 
it would be beneficial for an agreement to be entered into in the future for the project and there is 
no risk for the City with the resolution as the City will not be providing any funding.  The resolution 
is acknowledgement that the Carbon Asset Network has a partnership with the City for the project.  
Marvin Nash, Senior Advisor, Carbon Asset Network, provided information on the following:  
City’s 2% share of donations which is 2% of total donation after 70% is paid to landowners, block 
chain and carbon assets, Cheyenne is the only city they are currently working with, environmental 
social governance and certification that will be provided by the City which would acknowledge 
soil samples are sent to laboratories.  Mr. Nash further explained soil samples can’t be 
contaminated as they go through the block chain and don’t go back to the Carbon Asset Network 
and after the certification, a token is issued by the Carbon Asset Network. Upon inquiry, Mr. Nash 
advised in 2021, $40,000 was collected in fundraising efforts in 3 months and then the decision 
was made to stop fundraising which will be reapproached in 2022.  He explained that Encore Green 
Environmental is a 501c3 business, but the Carbon Asset Network is not.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by deleting the entirety of the current 
“Now Therefore, Be it Resolved” paragraph and by replacing with the following language: “NOW, 
THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne encourages and 
authorizes the Mayor and his administration to work with the Carbon Asset Network to develop a 
program in which the City will verify the Carbon Asset Network’s soil organic carbon and soil 
health protocol for receipt of two percent of donations made to the Carbon Asset Network.  And 
Furthermore, any agreements, MOU’s or contract s developed between the City and the Carbon 
Asset Network will be submitted to the Governing Body for approval.”, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  
Comments were made on the following topics: complexity of the resolution and the soil organic 
carbon capture project, further instruction and information needed for the path forward, support 
for the amendment, support for the resolution generally and concern of not going through the 
Request for Proposals (RFP) process.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.  Discussion continued and included the following: concern that the Carbon 
Asset Network does not sign the resolution, support for going through the RFP process, further 
support for the amendment, need for contracts/agreements in the future for the project and the 
complexity of organic carbon capture.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. J ohnson voting “no”.  
(#6202)

seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

Settlement Memorandum of Agreement – a distribution plan to resolve the City of Cheyenne’s 
claims in a  lawsuit regarding the Nation Opioid Epidemic.   Mr. Roybal advised the item was 
reviewed under other items for the agenda at the 12 -20-2021 Finance Committee meeting, 
therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, it was noted the City will receive $415,740.00 which will be 
distributed in annual payments of $23,096.66.  City Attorney Stefanie Boster, upon inquiry, 
explained the funds are required to be used for opioid abatement strategies that are listed in Exhibit 
A of the OneWyo Opioid Settlement Agreement.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. (#6204)
